<div class="shortdescription nomobile noexcerpt noprint searchaux" style="display:none">Country in Eastern Europe</div>

<p><b>Moldova</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="&#39;m&#39; in &#39;my&#39;">m</span><span title="/ɒ/: &#39;o&#39; in &#39;body&#39;">ɒ</span><span title="&#39;l&#39; in &#39;lie&#39;">l</span><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;d&#39; in &#39;dye&#39;">d</span><span title="/oʊ/: &#39;o&#39; in &#39;code&#39;">oʊ</span><span title="&#39;v&#39; in &#39;vie&#39;">v</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span></span>/</a></span>&#32;<span class="nowrap" style="font-size:85%">(<span class="unicode haudio"><span class="fn"><span style="white-space:nowrap;margin-right:.25em;"><a href="/wiki/File:En-us-Moldova.ogg" title="About this sound"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/11px-Loudspeaker.svg.png" decoding="async" width="11" height="11" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/17px-Loudspeaker.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/22px-Loudspeaker.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="20" data-file-height="20" /></a></span><a href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/d/df/En-us-Moldova.ogg" class="internal" title="En-us-Moldova.ogg">listen</a></span></span>)</span></span> <a href="/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key" title="Help:Pronunciation respelling key"><i title="English pronunciation respelling">mol-<span style="font-size:90%">DOH</span>-və</i></a>, <span style="font-size:85%;">sometimes </span><span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><small><a href="/wiki/British_English" title="British English">UK</a>: </small><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;m&#39; in &#39;my&#39;">m</span><span title="/ɒ/: &#39;o&#39; in &#39;body&#39;">ɒ</span><span title="&#39;l&#39; in &#39;lie&#39;">l</span><span title="&#39;d&#39; in &#39;dye&#39;">d</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span><span title="&#39;v&#39; in &#39;vie&#39;">v</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span></span>/</a></span></span> <a href="/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key" title="Help:Pronunciation respelling key"><i title="English pronunciation respelling"><span style="font-size:90%">MOL</span>-də-və</i></a>;<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> <small>Romanian pronunciation:&#160;</small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="ro-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Romanian" title="Help:IPA/Romanian">[molˈdova]</a></span>), officially the <b>Republic of Moldova</b> (<a href="/wiki/Romanian_language" title="Romanian language">Romanian</a>: <i lang="ro">Republica Moldova</i>), is a <a href="/wiki/Landlocked_country" title="Landlocked country">landlocked</a> country in <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Europe" title="Eastern Europe">Eastern Europe</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> It is bordered by <a href="/wiki/Romania" title="Romania">Romania</a> to the west and <a href="/wiki/Ukraine" title="Ukraine">Ukraine</a> to the north, east, and south.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook_Moldova_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook_Moldova-21">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/List_of_states_with_limited_recognition" title="List of states with limited recognition">unrecognised state</a> of <a href="/wiki/Transnistria" title="Transnistria">Transnistria</a> lies across the <a href="/wiki/Dniester" title="Dniester">Dniester</a> river on the country's eastern border with Ukraine. Moldova's <a href="/wiki/Capital_city" title="Capital city">capital</a> and largest city is <a href="/wiki/Chi%C8%99in%C4%83u" title="Chișinău">Chișinău</a>.
</p><p>Most of Moldovan territory was a part of the <a href="/wiki/Principality_of_Moldavia" class="mw-redirect" title="Principality of Moldavia">Principality of Moldavia</a> from the 14th century until 1812, when it was <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Bucharest_(1812)" title="Treaty of Bucharest (1812)">ceded</a> to the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Empire" title="Russian Empire">Russian Empire</a> by the <a href="/wiki/Ottoman_Empire" title="Ottoman Empire">Ottoman Empire</a> (to which Moldavia was a <a href="/wiki/Vassal_and_tributary_states_of_the_Ottoman_Empire" title="Vassal and tributary states of the Ottoman Empire">vassal state</a>) and became known as <a href="/wiki/Bessarabia" title="Bessarabia">Bessarabia</a>. In 1856, southern Bessarabia was returned to Moldavia, which three years later united with <a href="/wiki/Wallachia" title="Wallachia">Wallachia</a> to form <a href="/wiki/United_Principalities_of_Moldavia_and_Wallachia" title="United Principalities of Moldavia and Wallachia">Romania</a>, but Russian rule was restored over the whole of the region in 1878. During the 1917 <a href="/wiki/Russian_Revolution" title="Russian Revolution">Russian Revolution</a>, Bessarabia briefly became an <a href="/wiki/Moldavian_Democratic_Republic" title="Moldavian Democratic Republic">autonomous state</a> within the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Republic" title="Russian Republic">Russian Republic</a>. In February 1918, it declared independence and then integrated into Romania later that year following a vote of its assembly. The decision was disputed by <a href="/wiki/Russian_Soviet_Federative_Socialist_Republic" title="Russian Soviet Federative Socialist Republic">Soviet Russia</a>, which in 1924 established, within the <a href="/wiki/Ukrainian_Soviet_Socialist_Republic" title="Ukrainian Soviet Socialist Republic">Ukrainian SSR</a>, a so-called <a href="/wiki/Moldavian_Autonomous_Soviet_Socialist_Republic" title="Moldavian Autonomous Soviet Socialist Republic">Moldavian autonomous republic</a> on partially Moldovan-inhabited territories to the east of Bessarabia.
</p><p>In 1940, as a consequence of the <a href="/wiki/Molotov%E2%80%93Ribbentrop_Pact" title="Molotov–Ribbentrop Pact">Molotov–Ribbentrop Pact</a>, Romania was <a href="/wiki/Soviet_occupation_of_Bessarabia_and_Northern_Bukovina" class="mw-redirect" title="Soviet occupation of Bessarabia and Northern Bukovina">compelled to cede Bessarabia and Northern Bukovina</a> to the <a href="/wiki/Soviet_Union" title="Soviet Union">Soviet Union</a>, leading to the creation of the <a href="/wiki/Moldavian_Soviet_Socialist_Republic" title="Moldavian Soviet Socialist Republic">Moldavian Soviet Socialist Republic</a> (Moldavian SSR). On 27 August 1991, as the <a href="/wiki/Dissolution_of_the_Soviet_Union" title="Dissolution of the Soviet Union">dissolution of the Soviet Union</a> was underway, the Moldavian SSR <a href="/wiki/Moldovan_Declaration_of_Independence" title="Moldovan Declaration of Independence">declared independence</a> and took the name Moldova.<sup id="cite_ref-natura2000_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-natura2000-22">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> However, the strip of Moldovan territory on the east bank of the Dniester has been under the <i><a href="/wiki/De_facto" title="De facto">de facto</a></i> control of the breakaway government of Transnistria since 1990. The <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Moldova" title="Constitution of Moldova">constitution of Moldova</a> was adopted in 1994, and the country became a <a href="/wiki/Parliamentary_republic" title="Parliamentary republic">parliamentary republic</a> with a <a href="/wiki/President_of_Moldova" title="President of Moldova">president</a> as <a href="/wiki/Head_of_state" title="Head of state">head of state</a> and a <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Moldova" title="Prime Minister of Moldova">prime minister</a> as <a href="/wiki/Head_of_government" title="Head of government">head of government</a>.
</p><p>Moldova is the <a href="/wiki/List_of_sovereign_states_in_Europe_by_GDP_(PPP)_per_capita" title="List of sovereign states in Europe by GDP (PPP) per capita">second poorest country in Europe by GDP per official capita</a> after Ukraine and much of its <a href="/wiki/Gross_domestic_product" title="Gross domestic product">GDP</a> is dominated by the <a href="/wiki/Service_sector" class="mw-redirect" title="Service sector">service sector</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> It has one of the lowest <a href="/wiki/Human_Development_Index" title="Human Development Index">Human Development Indexes</a> in Europe, ranking 76th in the world (2022).<sup id="cite_ref-HDI_16-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-HDI-16">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> Moldova is a member state of the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Council_of_Europe" title="Council of Europe">Council of Europe</a>, the <a href="/wiki/World_Trade_Organization" title="World Trade Organization">World Trade Organization</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Organization_for_Security_and_Cooperation_in_Europe" class="mw-redirect" title="Organization for Security and Cooperation in Europe">Organization for Security and Cooperation in Europe</a>, the <a href="/wiki/GUAM_Organization_for_Democracy_and_Economic_Development" title="GUAM Organization for Democracy and Economic Development">GUAM Organization for Democracy and Economic Development</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Independent_States" title="Commonwealth of Independent States">Commonwealth of Independent States</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Organization_of_the_Black_Sea_Economic_Cooperation" title="Organization of the Black Sea Economic Cooperation">Organization of the Black Sea Economic Cooperation</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Association_Trio" title="Association Trio">Association Trio</a>. Moldova has been an <a href="/wiki/Potential_enlargement_of_the_European_Union" title="Potential enlargement of the European Union">official candidate</a> for membership in the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a> since June 2022.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Etymology">Etymology</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Names_of_Moldavia_and_Moldova" title="Names of Moldavia and Moldova">Names of Moldavia and Moldova</a></div>
<p>The name <i>Moldova</i> is derived from the <a href="/wiki/Moldova_(river)" title="Moldova (river)">Moldova River</a> (<a href="/wiki/German_language" title="German language">German</a>: <i lang="de">Moldau</i>); the valley of this river served as a political centre at the time of the <a href="/wiki/Founding_of_Moldavia" title="Founding of Moldavia">foundation</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Moldavia" title="Moldavia">Principality of Moldavia</a> in 1359.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> The origin of the name of the river remains unclear. According to a legend recounted by Moldavian chroniclers <a href="/wiki/Dimitrie_Cantemir" title="Dimitrie Cantemir">Dimitrie Cantemir</a> and <a href="/wiki/Grigore_Ureche" title="Grigore Ureche">Grigore Ureche</a>, Prince <a href="/wiki/Drago%C8%99,_Voivode_of_Moldavia" title="Dragoș, Voivode of Moldavia">Dragoș</a> named the river after hunting <a href="/wiki/Aurochs" title="Aurochs">aurochs</a>: following the chase, the prince's exhausted hound <i>Molda (Seva)</i> was drowned in the river. The dog's name, given to the river, extended to the principality.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>For a short time in the 1990s, at the founding of the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Independent_States" title="Commonwealth of Independent States">Commonwealth of Independent States</a>, the name of the current Republic of Moldova was also spelled <i>Moldavia</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> After the <a href="/wiki/Dissolution_of_the_Soviet_Union" title="Dissolution of the Soviet Union">dissolution of the Soviet Union</a>, the country began to use the Romanian name, <i>Moldova</i>. Officially, the name <i>Republic of Moldova</i> is designated by the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Moldova" title="History of Moldova">History of Moldova</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Transnistria" title="History of Transnistria">History of Transnistria</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Prehistory">Prehistory</span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:CuTryOutline.svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/CuTryOutline.svg/220px-CuTryOutline.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="184" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/CuTryOutline.svg/330px-CuTryOutline.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/CuTryOutline.svg/440px-CuTryOutline.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="938" data-file-height="786" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:CuTryOutline.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Extent of the <a href="/wiki/Cucuteni%E2%80%93Trypillia_culture" title="Cucuteni–Trypillia culture">Cucuteni–Trypillia culture</a>, <abbr title="circa">c.</abbr> 4,000 BC</div></div></div>
<p>The prehistory of Moldova covers the period from the <a href="/wiki/Upper_Paleolithic" title="Upper Paleolithic">Upper Paleolithic</a> which begins with the presence of <a href="/wiki/Cro-Magnon" class="mw-redirect" title="Cro-Magnon">Homo sapiens</a> in the area of <a href="/wiki/Prehistory_of_Southeastern_Europe" title="Prehistory of Southeastern Europe">Southeastern Europe</a> some 44,000 years ago and extends into the appearance of the first written records in <a href="/wiki/Classical_Antiquity" class="mw-redirect" title="Classical Antiquity">Classical Antiquity</a> in <a href="/wiki/Ancient_Greece" title="Ancient Greece">Greece</a>. In 2010, <a href="/wiki/Oldowan" title="Oldowan">Oldowan</a> flint tools were discovered at Bayraki that are 800,000–1.2 million years old.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> During the <a href="/wiki/Neolithic" title="Neolithic">Neolithic Age</a>, Moldova's territory stood at the centre of the large <a href="/wiki/Cucuteni%E2%80%93Trypillia_culture" title="Cucuteni–Trypillia culture">Cucuteni–Trypillia culture</a> that stretched east beyond the <a href="/wiki/Dniester_River" class="mw-redirect" title="Dniester River">Dniester River</a> in Ukraine and west up to and beyond the <a href="/wiki/Carpathian_Mountains" title="Carpathian Mountains">Carpathian Mountains</a> in Romania. The people of this civilization, which lasted roughly from 5500 to 2750 BC, practised agriculture, raised livestock, hunted, and made intricately designed pottery.<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Antiquity_and_the_early_Middle_Ages">Antiquity and the early Middle Ages</span></h3>
<p>This area of present-day Moldova was inhabited by ancient <a href="/wiki/Dacia" title="Dacia">Dacians</a> and Moldovans identify themselves with their ancestors. <a href="/wiki/Carpian" class="mw-redirect" title="Carpian">Carpian</a> tribes also inhabited Moldova's territory in the period of <a href="/wiki/Classical_antiquity" title="Classical antiquity">classical antiquity</a>. Between the first and seventh centuries AD, the south came intermittently under the control of the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Empire" title="Roman Empire">Roman</a> and then the <a href="/wiki/Byzantine_Empire" title="Byzantine Empire">Byzantine Empires</a>. Due to its strategic location on a route between Asia and Europe, the territory of modern Moldova experienced many invasions in late antiquity and the <a href="/wiki/Early_Middle_Ages" title="Early Middle Ages">Early Middle Ages</a>, notably by <a href="/wiki/Goths" title="Goths">Goths</a>, <a href="/wiki/Huns" title="Huns">Huns</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pannonian_Avars" title="Pannonian Avars">Avars</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bulgars" title="Bulgars">Bulgars</a>, <a href="/wiki/Magyars" class="mw-redirect" title="Magyars">Magyars</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pechenegs" title="Pechenegs">Pechenegs</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cumans" title="Cumans">Cumans</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mongol_invasion_of_Europe" title="Mongol invasion of Europe">Mongols</a> and <a href="/wiki/Mongol_and_Tatar_states_in_Europe" class="mw-redirect" title="Mongol and Tatar states in Europe">Tatars</a>.
</p><p>In the 11th century, a <a href="/wiki/Viking" class="mw-redirect" title="Viking">Viking</a> by the name of Rodfos was possibly killed in the area by the <a href="/wiki/Blakumen" title="Blakumen">Blakumen</a> who betrayed him.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> In 1164, the future <a href="/wiki/Byzantine_Empire" title="Byzantine Empire">Byzantine emperor</a> <a href="/wiki/Andronikos_I_Komnenos" title="Andronikos I Komnenos">Andronikos I Komnenos</a>, while attempting to reach the <a href="/wiki/Principality_of_Halych" title="Principality of Halych">Principality of Halych</a>, was taken prisoner by Vlachs, possibly in the area which now constitutes Moldova.
</p><p>The East Slavic <a href="/wiki/Hypatian_Chronicle" class="mw-redirect" title="Hypatian Chronicle">Hypatian Chronicle</a> (13th century) mentions the <a href="/wiki/Bolokhoveni" class="mw-redirect" title="Bolokhoveni">Bolohoveni</a> people, who resided on the eastern fringes of Moldovan territory and in the <a href="/wiki/Rus%27_principalities" class="mw-redirect" title="Rus&#39; principalities">Rus' principalities</a> of <a href="/wiki/Principality_of_Halych" title="Principality of Halych">Halych</a>, <a href="/wiki/Principality_of_Volhynia" title="Principality of Volhynia">Volhynia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Principality_of_Kyiv" class="mw-redirect" title="Principality of Kyiv">Kyiv</a>; their ethnic origin is disputed by historians. Archaeological research has identified the location of 13th-century fortified settlements in this region.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> The Bolohoveni disappeared from written chronicles after they were defeated in 1257 by <a href="/wiki/Daniel_of_Galicia" title="Daniel of Galicia">Daniel of Galicia</a>. In the early 13th century, the <a href="/wiki/Brodnici" title="Brodnici"><i>Brodniks</i></a>, a possible <a href="/wiki/Slavs" title="Slavs">Slavic</a>&#8211;<a href="/wiki/Vlachs" title="Vlachs">Vlach</a> <a href="/wiki/Vassal" title="Vassal">vassal</a> tribe of <a href="/wiki/Halych-Volhynia" class="mw-redirect" title="Halych-Volhynia">Halych</a>, were also present in much of the region's territory.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Founding_of_the_Principality_of_Moldavia">Founding of the Principality of Moldavia</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Founding_of_Moldavia" title="Founding of Moldavia">Founding of Moldavia</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:177px;"><a href="/wiki/File:DragosIofMoldavia.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/DragosIofMoldavia.jpg/175px-DragosIofMoldavia.jpg" decoding="async" width="175" height="260" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/DragosIofMoldavia.jpg/263px-DragosIofMoldavia.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/DragosIofMoldavia.jpg/350px-DragosIofMoldavia.jpg 2x" data-file-width="730" data-file-height="1084" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:DragosIofMoldavia.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Drago%C8%99" class="mw-redirect" title="Dragoș">Dragoș</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Vlach" class="mw-redirect" title="Vlach">Vlach</a> <a href="/wiki/Voivode" title="Voivode">voivode</a> and founder of the <a href="/wiki/Founding_of_Moldavia" title="Founding of Moldavia">Principality of Moldavia</a>, 19th-century depiction</div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Founding_of_Moldavia" title="Founding of Moldavia">Principality of Moldavia</a> began when a <a href="/wiki/Vlach" class="mw-redirect" title="Vlach">Vlach</a> <a href="/wiki/Voivode" title="Voivode">voivode</a> (military leader), <a href="/wiki/Drago%C8%99" class="mw-redirect" title="Dragoș">Dragoș</a>, arrived in the region of the <a href="/wiki/Moldova_River" class="mw-redirect" title="Moldova River">Moldova River</a>. His people from the <a href="/wiki/Voivodeship_of_Maramure%C8%99" title="Voivodeship of Maramureș">voivodeship</a> at <a href="/wiki/Maramure%C8%99" title="Maramureș">Maramureș</a> soon followed. Dragoș established a <a href="/wiki/Polity" title="Polity">polity</a> as a vassal to the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary" title="Kingdom of Hungary">Kingdom of Hungary</a> in the 1350s. The independence of the <a href="/wiki/Principality" title="Principality">Principality</a> of <a href="/wiki/Moldavia" title="Moldavia">Moldavia</a> came when <a href="/wiki/Bogdan_I_of_Moldavia" title="Bogdan I of Moldavia">Bogdan I</a>, another Vlach voivode from Maramureș who had fallen out with the Hungarian king, crossed the <a href="/wiki/Carpathian_mountains" class="mw-redirect" title="Carpathian mountains">Carpathian mountains</a> in 1359 and took control of Moldavia, wresting the region from Hungary. The Principality of <a href="/wiki/Moldavia" title="Moldavia">Moldavia</a> was bounded by the <a href="/wiki/Carpathian_Mountains" title="Carpathian Mountains">Carpathian Mountains</a> in the west, the <a href="/wiki/Dniester" title="Dniester">Dniester</a> River in the east, and the Danube River and <a href="/wiki/Black_Sea" title="Black Sea">Black Sea</a> to the south. Its territory comprised the present-day territory of the Republic of Moldova, the <a href="/wiki/Western_Moldavia" title="Western Moldavia">eastern eight counties</a> of Romania, and parts of the <a href="/wiki/Chernivtsi_Oblast" title="Chernivtsi Oblast">Chernivtsi Oblast</a> and <a href="/wiki/Budjak" title="Budjak">Budjak</a> region of present-day Ukraine. Locals referred to the principality as <i>Moldova</i> - like the present-day republic and Romania's north-eastern region.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Between_Poland_and_Hungary">Between Poland and Hungary</span></h3>
<p>The history of what is today Moldova has been intertwined with that of Poland for centuries. The Polish chronicler <a href="/wiki/Jan_D%C5%82ugosz" title="Jan Długosz">Jan Długosz</a> mentioned Moldavians as having joined a military expedition in 1342, under King <a href="/wiki/W%C5%82adys%C5%82aw_I_the_Elbow-high" class="mw-redirect" title="Władysław I the Elbow-high">Ladislaus I</a>, against the <a href="/wiki/Margraviate_of_Brandenburg" title="Margraviate of Brandenburg">Margraviate of Brandenburg</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> The Polish state was powerful enough to counter the <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_Kingdom" class="mw-redirect" title="Hungarian Kingdom">Hungarian Kingdom</a> which was consistently interested in bringing the area that would become Moldavia into its political orbit.
</p><p>Ties between Poland and Moldavia expanded after the founding of the Moldavian state by <a href="/wiki/Bogdan_I_of_Moldavia" title="Bogdan I of Moldavia">Bogdan of Cuhea</a>, a Vlach <a href="/wiki/Voivode" title="Voivode">voivode</a> from <a href="/wiki/Maramure%C8%99" title="Maramureș">Maramureș</a> who had fallen out with the Hungarian king. Crossing the <a href="/wiki/Carpathian_mountains" class="mw-redirect" title="Carpathian mountains">Carpathian mountains</a> in 1359, the voivode took control of Moldavia and succeeded in creating Moldavia as an independent political entity. Despite being disfavored by the brief union of <a href="/wiki/History_of_Poland_during_the_Piast_dynasty" title="History of Poland during the Piast dynasty">Angevin Poland</a> and Hungary (the latter was still the country's overlord), Bogdan's successor <a href="/wiki/La%C8%9Bcu_of_Moldavia" title="Lațcu of Moldavia">Lațcu</a>, the Moldavian ruler also likely allied himself with the <a href="/wiki/Polish_people" title="Polish people">Poles</a>. Lațcu also accepted <a href="/wiki/Religious_conversion" title="Religious conversion">conversion</a> to <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Roman Catholicism</a> around 1370, but his gesture was to remain without consequences.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Polish_influence_grows">Polish influence grows</span></h4>
<p><a href="/wiki/Petru_I_of_Moldavia" title="Petru I of Moldavia">Petru I</a> profited from the end of the Polish-Hungarian union and moved the country closer to the <a href="/wiki/History_of_Poland_during_the_Jagiellon_dynasty" class="mw-redirect" title="History of Poland during the Jagiellon dynasty">Jagiellon realm</a>, becoming a <a href="/wiki/Vassalage" class="mw-redirect" title="Vassalage">vassal</a> of king <a href="/wiki/W%C5%82adys%C5%82aw_II_Jagie%C5%82%C5%82o" title="Władysław II Jagiełło">Jogaila of Poland</a> on 26 September 1387. This gesture was to have unexpected consequences: Petru supplied the Polish ruler with funds needed in the war against the <a href="/wiki/Teutonic_Knights" class="mw-redirect" title="Teutonic Knights">Teutonic Knights</a>, and was granted control over <a href="/wiki/Pokuttya" class="mw-redirect" title="Pokuttya">Pokuttya</a> until the debt was to be repaid; as this is not recorded to have been carried out, the region became disputed by the two states, until it was lost by Moldavia in the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Obertyn" title="Battle of Obertyn">Battle of Obertyn</a> (1531). Prince Petru also expanded his rule southwards to the <a href="/wiki/Danube_Delta" title="Danube Delta">Danube Delta</a>. His brother Roman I conquered the Hungarian-ruled <a href="/wiki/Bilhorod-Dnistrovskyi" title="Bilhorod-Dnistrovskyi">Cetatea Albă</a> in 1392, giving Moldavia an outlet to the <a href="/wiki/Black_Sea" title="Black Sea">Black Sea</a>, before being toppled from the throne for supporting <a href="/wiki/Fyodor_Koriatovych" title="Fyodor Koriatovych">Fyodor Koriatovych</a> in his conflict with <a href="/wiki/Vytautas_the_Great" class="mw-redirect" title="Vytautas the Great">Vytautas the Great</a> of <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duchy_of_Lithuania" title="Grand Duchy of Lithuania">Lithuania</a>. Under <a href="/wiki/Stephen_I_of_Moldavia" title="Stephen I of Moldavia">Stephen I</a>, growing Polish influence was challenged by <a href="/wiki/Sigismund,_Holy_Roman_Emperor" title="Sigismund, Holy Roman Emperor">Sigismund of Hungary</a>, whose expedition was defeated at <a href="/wiki/Ghind%C4%83oani" title="Ghindăoani">Ghindăoani</a> in 1385; however, Stephen disappeared in mysterious circumstances.
</p><p>Although <a href="/wiki/Alexander_I_of_Moldavia" title="Alexander I of Moldavia">Alexander I</a> was brought to the throne in 1400 by the Hungarians (with assistance from <a href="/wiki/Mircea_I_of_Wallachia" class="mw-redirect" title="Mircea I of Wallachia">Mircea I of Wallachia</a>), this ruler shifted his allegiances towards Poland (notably engaging Moldavian forces on the Polish side in the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Grunwald" title="Battle of Grunwald">Battle of Grunwald</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Siege_of_Marienburg_(1410)" title="Siege of Marienburg (1410)">siege of Marienburg</a>), and placed his own choice of rulers in Wallachia. His reign was one of the most successful in Moldavia's history.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Increasing_Ottoman_influence">Increasing Ottoman influence</span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:%D0%A1%D0%BE%D1%80%D0%BE%D0%BA%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%B0%D1%8F_%D0%BA%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%BF%D0%BE%D1%81%D1%82%D1%8C_Cetatea_Soroca_Soroca_Fortress_(44738806701).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/25/%D0%A1%D0%BE%D1%80%D0%BE%D0%BA%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%B0%D1%8F_%D0%BA%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%BF%D0%BE%D1%81%D1%82%D1%8C_Cetatea_Soroca_Soroca_Fortress_%2844738806701%29.jpg/220px-%D0%A1%D0%BE%D1%80%D0%BE%D0%BA%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%B0%D1%8F_%D0%BA%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%BF%D0%BE%D1%81%D1%82%D1%8C_Cetatea_Soroca_Soroca_Fortress_%2844738806701%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="138" class="thumbimage"
