/></a></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center"><a href="/wiki/Dorin_Recean" title="Dorin Recean">Dorin Recean</a><br /><small><a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Moldova" title="Prime Minister of Moldova">Prime Minister</a></small></div></div></div></div></div>
<p>In the <a href="/wiki/2020_Moldovan_presidential_election" title="2020 Moldovan presidential election">November 2020 presidential election</a>, the pro-European opposition candidate <a href="/wiki/Maia_Sandu" title="Maia Sandu">Maia Sandu</a> was elected as the new president of the republic, defeating incumbent pro-Russian president Igor Dodon and thus becoming the first female elected president of Moldova.<sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> In December 2020, Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Ion_Chicu" title="Ion Chicu">Ion Chicu</a>, who had led a pro-Russian government since November 2019, resigned a day before Sandu was sworn in.<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> The parliament, dominated by pro-Russian Socialists, did not accept any Prime Minister candidate proposed by the new president.<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> On 28 April 2021, Sandu dissolved the <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_the_Republic_of_Moldova" class="mw-redirect" title="Parliament of the Republic of Moldova">Parliament of the Republic of Moldova</a> after the <a href="/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Moldova" title="Constitutional Court of Moldova">Constitutional Court</a> ended Moldova's <a href="/wiki/State_of_emergency" title="State of emergency">state of emergency</a> which had been brought about by the <a href="/wiki/Coronavirus_pandemic" class="mw-redirect" title="Coronavirus pandemic">coronavirus pandemic</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> Parliamentary elections <a href="/wiki/2021_Moldovan_parliamentary_election" title="2021 Moldovan parliamentary election">took place</a> on 11 July 2021.<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> The snap parliamentary elections resulted in a landslide win for the pro-European <a href="/wiki/Party_of_Action_and_Solidarity" title="Party of Action and Solidarity">Party of Action and Solidarity</a> (PAS).<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> On 6 August 2021, the <a href="/wiki/Natalia_Gavrili%C8%9Ba" title="Natalia Gavrilița">Natalia Gavrilița</a>-led <a href="/wiki/Gavrili%C8%9Ba_Cabinet" title="Gavrilița Cabinet">cabinet</a> was sworn in to office with 61 votes, all from the <a href="/wiki/Party_of_Action_and_Solidarity" title="Party of Action and Solidarity">Party of Action and Solidarity</a> (PAS).<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> She resigned on 10 February 2023.<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Russian_invasion_of_neighbouring_Ukraine">Russian invasion of neighbouring Ukraine</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/2022_Transnistria_attacks" title="2022 Transnistria attacks">2022 Transnistria attacks</a>, <a href="/wiki/2022%E2%80%932023_Moldovan_energy_crisis" title="2022–2023 Moldovan energy crisis">2022–2023 Moldovan energy crisis</a>, and <a href="/wiki/2022%E2%80%932023_Moldovan_protests" class="mw-redirect" title="2022–2023 Moldovan protests">2022–2023 Moldovan protests</a></div>
<p>In February 2022 Sandu condemned the <a href="/wiki/2022_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine" class="mw-redirect" title="2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine">Russian invasion of Ukraine</a>, calling it "a blatant breach of international law and of Ukrainian sovereignty and territorial integrity."<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> Prime Minister Natalia Gavrilita stated on 28 February 2022 that Moldova should rapidly move to become a member of the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> the country submitted a formal application for EU membership on 3 March 2022,<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> and was subsequently granted the status of <a href="/wiki/European_Union#Candidate_countries" title="European Union">candidate country</a> by the <a href="/wiki/European_Council" title="European Council">European Council</a> on 23 June 2022<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> along with Ukraine.
</p><p>On 26 April 2022, authorities from the Transnistria region said two transmitting antennas broadcasting Russian radio programs at <a href="/wiki/Grigoriopol_transmitter" title="Grigoriopol transmitter">Grigoriopol transmitter</a> broadcasting facility near the town of <a href="/wiki/Maiac" title="Maiac">Maiac</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Grigoriopol_District" title="Grigoriopol District">Grigoriopol District</a> near the Ukrainian border <a href="/wiki/2022_Transnistria_attacks" title="2022 Transnistria attacks">had been blown up</a> and the previous evening, the premises of the <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_State_Security_(Transnistria)" title="Ministry of State Security (Transnistria)">Transnistrian state security service</a> had been attacked.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_110-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-110">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup> The Russian army has a military base and a large ammunition dump in the region. Russia has about 1,500 soldiers stationed in breakaway Transnistria. They are supposed to serve there as peacekeepers.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_110-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-110">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 24 May 2022, the former president of Moldova, Igor Dodon, was arrested. Dodon, leader of Moldova's main pro-Russian opposition, the Socialist Party, was accused of taking bribes. Moldova's pro-Western and pro-Russian factions became increasingly divided since Russia's invasion of Ukraine.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 31 October 2022, Moldova's Interior Ministry said that debris from a Russian missile landed in the northern village of <a href="/wiki/Naslavcea" title="Naslavcea">Naslavcea</a> after a Russian fusillade was intercepted by air defenses in neighboring Ukraine. The Ministry reported no people were hurt but the windows of several residential homes were shattered. The Russian strike was targeting a Ukrainian dam on the <a href="/wiki/Dniester_river" class="mw-redirect" title="Dniester river">Nistru river</a> that runs through Moldova and Ukraine.<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> On 5 December, another missile fell near the city of <a href="/wiki/Briceni" title="Briceni">Briceni</a> as Russia launched another wave of missile strikes against Ukraine.<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> Yet another missile fell into <a href="/wiki/Larga,_Briceni" title="Larga, Briceni">Larga</a> on 14 January 2023 as a result of another wave of missile strikes against Ukraine.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Government">Government</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Moldova" title="Government of Moldova">Government of Moldova</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Parliament_Building_in_Chi%C8%99in%C4%83u.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Parliament_Building_in_Chi%C8%99in%C4%83u.jpg/220px-Parliament_Building_in_Chi%C8%99in%C4%83u.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="157" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Parliament_Building_in_Chi%C8%99in%C4%83u.jpg/330px-Parliament_Building_in_Chi%C8%99in%C4%83u.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Parliament_Building_in_Chi%C8%99in%C4%83u.jpg/440px-Parliament_Building_in_Chi%C8%99in%C4%83u.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2775" data-file-height="1975" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Parliament_Building_in_Chi%C8%99in%C4%83u.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Moldovan_Parliament" class="mw-redirect" title="Moldovan Parliament">Moldovan Parliament</a></div></div></div>
<p>Moldova is a <a href="/wiki/Unitary_state" title="Unitary state">unitary</a> <a href="/wiki/Parliamentary_system" title="Parliamentary system">parliamentary</a> <a href="/wiki/Representative_democracy" title="Representative democracy">representative</a> democratic <a href="/wiki/Republic" title="Republic">republic</a>. The 1994 Constitution of Moldova sets the framework for the government of the country. A parliamentary majority of at least two-thirds is required to amend the <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Moldova" title="Constitution of Moldova">Constitution of Moldova</a>, which cannot be revised in times of war or national emergency. Amendments to the Constitution affecting the state's sovereignty, independence, or unity can only be made after a majority of voters support the proposal in a referendum. Furthermore, no revision can be made to limit the fundamental rights of people enumerated in the Constitution.<sup id="cite_ref-const_116-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-const-116">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> The country's central legislative body is the <a href="/wiki/Unicameral" class="mw-redirect" title="Unicameral">unicameral</a> <a href="/wiki/Moldovan_Parliament" class="mw-redirect" title="Moldovan Parliament">Moldovan Parliament</a> (<i><span title="Romanian-language text"><i lang="ro">Parlament</i></span></i>), which has 101 seats, and whose members are elected by popular vote on <a href="/wiki/Proportional_representation" title="Proportional representation">party lists</a> every four years.
</p><p>The head of state is the President of Moldova, who between 2001 and 2015 was elected by the Moldovan Parliament, requiring the support of three-fifths of the deputies (at least 61 votes). This system was designed to decrease executive authority in favour of the legislature. Nevertheless, the <a href="/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Moldova" title="Constitutional Court of Moldova">Constitutional Court</a> ruled on 4 March 2016, that this constitutional change adopted in 2000 regarding the presidential election was unconstitutional,<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> thus reverting the election method of the president to a <a href="/wiki/Two-round_system" title="Two-round system">two-round system</a> <a href="/wiki/Direct_election" title="Direct election">direct election</a>. The president appoints a <a href="/wiki/List_of_Prime_Ministers_of_Moldova" class="mw-redirect" title="List of Prime Ministers of Moldova">prime minister</a> who functions as the <a href="/wiki/Head_of_government" title="Head of government">head of government</a>, and who in turn assembles a <a href="/wiki/Cabinet_of_Moldova" title="Cabinet of Moldova">cabinet</a>, both subject to parliamentary approval.
</p><p>The 1994 constitution also establishes an <a href="/wiki/Judicial_independence" title="Judicial independence">independent</a> <a href="/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Moldova" title="Constitutional Court of Moldova">Constitutional Court</a>, composed of six judges (two appointed by the President, two by Parliament, and two by the Supreme Council of <a href="/wiki/Magistrate" title="Magistrate">Magistrature</a>), serving six-year terms, during which they are irremovable and not subordinate to any power. The court is invested with the power of <a href="/wiki/Judicial_review" title="Judicial review">judicial review</a> over all acts of parliament, over presidential decrees, and over international treaties signed by the country.<sup id="cite_ref-const_116-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-const-116">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Internal_affairs">Internal affairs</span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Ministry_of_Internal_Affairs_(AP4L0079_1PS)_(28921126420).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/18/Ministry_of_Internal_Affairs_%28AP4L0079_1PS%29_%2828921126420%29.jpg/220px-Ministry_of_Internal_Affairs_%28AP4L0079_1PS%29_%2828921126420%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/18/Ministry_of_Internal_Affairs_%28AP4L0079_1PS%29_%2828921126420%29.jpg/330px-Ministry_of_Internal_Affairs_%28AP4L0079_1PS%29_%2828921126420%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/18/Ministry_of_Internal_Affairs_%28AP4L0079_1PS%29_%2828921126420%29.jpg/440px-Ministry_of_Internal_Affairs_%28AP4L0079_1PS%29_%2828921126420%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3072" data-file-height="2304" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Ministry_of_Internal_Affairs_(AP4L0079_1PS)_(28921126420).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Internal_Affairs_(Moldova)" title="Ministry of Internal Affairs (Moldova)">Ministry of Internal Affairs of Moldova</a>, Chișinău</div></div></div>
<p>On 19 December 2016, Moldovan MPs approved raising the retirement age to 63 years<sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> from the current level of 57 for women and 62 for men, a reform that is part of a 3-year-old assistance program agreed with the <a href="/wiki/International_Monetary_Fund" title="International Monetary Fund">International Monetary Fund</a>. The retirement age will be lifted gradually by a few months every year until it is fully in effect in 2028.
</p><p>Life expectancy in the ex-Soviet country is 67.5 years for men and 75.5 years for women, both among the poorest in Europe. In a country with a population of 3.5 million, of which 1 million are abroad, there are more than 700,000 pensioners.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_relations">Foreign relations</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Moldova" title="Foreign relations of Moldova">Foreign relations of Moldova</a> and <a href="/wiki/Moldova%E2%80%93European_Union_relations" title="Moldova–European Union relations">Moldova–European Union relations</a></div><p>After achieving independence from the Soviet Union, Moldova's foreign policy was designed with a view to establishing relations with other European countries, neutrality, and European Union integration. In 1995 the country was admitted to the <a href="/wiki/Council_of_Europe" title="Council of Europe">Council of Europe</a>.
In addition to its participation in <a href="/wiki/NATO" title="NATO">NATO</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Partnership_for_Peace" title="Partnership for Peace">Partnership for Peace</a> programme, Moldova is also a member state of the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Organization_for_Security_and_Co-operation_in_Europe" title="Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe">Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe</a> (OSCE), the <a href="/wiki/North_Atlantic_Cooperation_Council" class="mw-redirect" title="North Atlantic Cooperation Council">North Atlantic Cooperation Council</a>, the <a href="/wiki/World_Trade_Organization" title="World Trade Organization">World Trade Organization</a>, the <a href="/wiki/International_Monetary_Fund" title="International Monetary Fund">International Monetary Fund</a>, the <a href="/wiki/World_Bank" title="World Bank">World Bank</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Organisation_internationale_de_la_Francophonie" title="Organisation internationale de la Francophonie">Francophonie</a> and the <a href="/wiki/European_Bank_for_Reconstruction_and_Development" title="European Bank for Reconstruction and Development">European Bank for Reconstruction and Development</a>.</p><div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:European_Union_Moldova_Locator.svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/European_Union_Moldova_Locator.svg/220px-European_Union_Moldova_Locator.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="225" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/European_Union_Moldova_Locator.svg/330px-European_Union_Moldova_Locator.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/European_Union_Moldova_Locator.svg/440px-European_Union_Moldova_Locator.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="593" data-file-height="606" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:European_Union_Moldova_Locator.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>In June 2022, Moldova became a recognised candidate for membership of the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>In 2005, Moldova and the European Union established an action plan that sought to improve cooperation between Moldova and the union. At the end of 2005, the <a href="/wiki/European_Union_Border_Assistance_Mission_to_Moldova_and_Ukraine" title="European Union Border Assistance Mission to Moldova and Ukraine">European Union Border Assistance Mission to Moldova and Ukraine</a> (EUBAM) was established at the joint request of the presidents of Moldova and Ukraine. EUBAM assists the Moldovan and Ukrainian governments in approximating their border and customs procedures to EU standards and offers support in both countries' fight against cross-border crime.
</p><p>After the 1990–1992 <a href="/wiki/War_of_Transnistria" class="mw-redirect" title="War of Transnistria">War of Transnistria</a>, Moldova sought a peaceful resolution to the conflict in the Transnistria region by working with Romania, Ukraine, and Russia, calling for international mediation, and co-operating with the OSCE and UN fact-finding and observer missions. The <a href="/wiki/Foreign_minister_of_Moldova" class="mw-redirect" title="Foreign minister of Moldova">foreign minister of Moldova</a>, <a href="/wiki/Andrei_Stratan" title="Andrei Stratan">Andrei Stratan</a>, repeatedly stated that the Russian troops stationed in the breakaway region were there against the will of the Moldovan government and called on them to leave "completely and unconditionally".<sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> In 2012, a <a href="/wiki/2012_Moldova_security_zone_incident" title="2012 Moldova security zone incident">security zone incident</a> resulted in the death of a civilian, raising tensions with Russia.<sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Batumi_International_Conference,_on_19_July_2021_05_(cropped).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/27/Batumi_International_Conference%2C_on_19_July_2021_05_%28cropped%29.jpg/220px-Batumi_International_Conference%2C_on_19_July_2021_05_%28cropped%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/27/Batumi_International_Conference%2C_on_19_July_2021_05_%28cropped%29.jpg/330px-Batumi_International_Conference%2C_on_19_July_2021_05_%28cropped%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/27/Batumi_International_Conference%2C_on_19_July_2021_05_%28cropped%29.jpg/440px-Batumi_International_Conference%2C_on_19_July_2021_05_%28cropped%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2548" data-file-height="1699" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Batumi_International_Conference,_on_19_July_2021_05_(cropped).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>President of Georgia <a href="/wiki/Salome_Zourabichvili" title="Salome Zourabichvili">Salome Zourabichvili</a>, President of Moldova <a href="/wiki/Maia_Sandu" title="Maia Sandu">Maia Sandu</a>, President of Ukraine <a href="/wiki/Volodymyr_Zelenskyy" title="Volodymyr Zelenskyy">Volodymyr Zelenskyy</a> and <a href="/wiki/President_of_the_European_Council" title="President of the European Council">President of the European Council</a> <a href="/wiki/Charles_Michel" title="Charles Michel">Charles Michel</a> during the 2021 <a href="/wiki/Batumi" title="Batumi">Batumi</a> International Conference. In 2014, the EU signed <a href="/wiki/European_Union_Association_Agreement" title="European Union Association Agreement">Association Agreements</a> with the three states.</div></div></div>
<p>In September 2010, the <a href="/wiki/European_Parliament" title="European Parliament">European Parliament</a> approved a grant of €90 million to Moldova.<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> The money was to supplement US$570 million in International Monetary Fund loans,<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> World Bank and other bilateral support already granted to Moldova. In April 2010, Romania offered Moldova development aid worth of €100 million while the number of scholarships for Moldovan students doubled to 5,000.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> According to a lending agreement signed in February 2010, Poland provided US$15 million as a component of its support for Moldova in its European integration efforts.<sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> The first joint meeting of the Governments of Romania and Moldova, held in March 2012, concluded with several bilateral agreements in various fields.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> The European orientation "has been the policy of Moldova in recent years and this is the policy that must continue," <a href="/wiki/Nicolae_Timofti" title="Nicolae Timofti">Nicolae Timofti</a> told lawmakers before <a href="/wiki/Moldovan_presidential_election,_2011%E2%80%932012" class="mw-redirect" title="Moldovan presidential election, 2011–2012">his election in 2012</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources#What_information_to_include" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources"><span title="A complete citation is needed. (December 2022)">full citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>On 29 November 2013, at a summit in Vilnius, Moldova signed an <a href="/wiki/European_Union_Association_Agreement" title="European Union Association Agreement">association agreement</a> with the European Union dedicated to the European Union's '<a href="/wiki/Eastern_Partnership" title="Eastern Partnership">Eastern Partnership</a>' with ex-Soviet countries.<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup> The ex-Romanian President <a href="/wiki/Traian_B%C4%83sescu" title="Traian Băsescu">Traian Băsescu</a> stated that Romania will make all efforts for Moldova to join the EU as soon as possible. Likewise, Traian Băsescu declared that the <a href="/wiki/Unification_of_Moldova_with_Romania" class="mw-redirect" title="Unification of Moldova with Romania">unification of Moldova with Romania</a> is the next national project for Romania, as more than 75% of the population speaks Romanian.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Moldova signed the Association Agreement with the European Union in Brussels on 27 June 2014. The signing came after the accord was drafted in Vilnius in November 2013.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Religious leaders play a role in shaping foreign policy. Since the fall of the Soviet Union, the Russian Government has frequently used its connections with the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Orthodox_Church" title="Russian Orthodox Church">Russian Orthodox Church</a> to block and stymie the integration of former Soviet states like Moldova into the West.<sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Moldova signed the membership application to join the EU on 3 March 2022.<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> On 23 June 2022, Moldova was officially granted <a href="/wiki/Potential_enlargement_of_the_European_Union" title="Potential enlargement of the European Union">candidate status</a> by EU leaders.<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Military">Military</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Military_of_Moldova" class="mw-redirect" title="Military of Moldova">Military of Moldova</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Moldovan_army_Capt._Deli_Ianec,_left,_role-playing_as_an_Afghan_National_Army_officer,_and_U.S._Army_Capt._Trey_Marsh,_with_Iron_Troop,_3rd_Squadron,_2nd_Cavalry_Regiment,_review_pictures_taken_after_a_search_130311-A-PU716-004.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/Moldovan_army_Capt._Deli_Ianec%2C_left%2C_role-playing_as_an_Afghan_National_Army_officer%2C_and_U.S._Army_Capt._Trey_Marsh%2C_with_Iron_Troop%2C_3rd_Squadron%2C_2nd_Cavalry_Regiment%2C_review_pictures_taken_after_a_search_130311-A-PU716-004.jpg/220px-thumbnail.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/Moldovan_army_Capt._Deli_Ianec%2C_left%2C_role-playing_as_an_Afghan_National_Army_officer%2C_and_U.S._Army_Capt._Trey_Marsh%2C_with_Iron_Troop%2C_3rd_Squadron%2C_2nd_Cavalry_Regiment%2C_review_pictures_taken_after_a_search_130311-A-PU716-004.jpg/330px-thumbnail.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/Moldovan_army_Capt._Deli_Ianec%2C_left%2C_role-playing_as_an_Afghan_National_Army_officer%2C_and_U.S._Army_Capt._Trey_Marsh%2C_with_Iron_Troop%2C_3rd_Squadron%2C_2nd_Cavalry_Regiment%2C_review_pictures_taken_after_a_search_130311-A-PU716-004.jpg/440px-thumbnail.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5616" data-file-height="3744" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Moldovan_army_Capt._Deli_Ianec,_left,_role-playing_as_an_Afghan_National_Army_officer,_and_U.S._Army_Capt._Trey_Marsh,_with_Iron_Troop,_3rd_Squadron,_2nd_Cavalry_Regiment,_review_pictures_taken_after_a_search_130311-A-PU716-004.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A soldier of the <a href="/wiki/Military_of_Moldova" class="mw-redirect" title="Military of Moldova">Moldovan Army</a> at the Joint Multinational Readiness Center in <a href="/wiki/Hohenfels,_Bavaria" title="Hohenfels, Bavaria">Hohenfels</a>, Germany</div></div></div>
<p>The Moldovan armed forces consists of the <a href="/wiki/Moldovan_Ground_Forces" title="Moldovan Ground Forces">Ground Forces</a> and <a href="/wiki/Moldovan_Air_Force" title="Moldovan Air Force">Air Force</a>. Moldova has accepted all relevant arms control obligations of the former Soviet Union. On 30 October 1992, Moldova ratified the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_on_Conventional_Armed_Forces_in_Europe" title="Treaty on Conventional Armed Forces in Europe">Treaty on Conventional Armed Forces in Europe</a>, which establishes comprehensive limits on key categories of conventional military equipment and provides for the destruction of weapons in excess of those limits. The country acceded to the provisions of the nuclear <a href="/wiki/Non-Proliferation_Treaty" class="mw-redirect" title="Non-Proliferation Treaty">Non-Proliferation Treaty</a> in October 1994 in <a href="/wiki/Washington,_D.C." title="Washington, D.C.">Washington, D.C.</a> It does not have nuclear, biological, chemical or radiological weapons. Moldova joined the <a href="/wiki/North_Atlantic_Treaty_Organization" class="mw-redirect" title="North Atlantic Treaty Organization">North Atlantic Treaty Organization</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Partnership_for_Peace" title="Partnership for Peace">Partnership for Peace</a> on 16 March 1994.
</p><p>Moldova is committed to a number of international and regional control of arms regulations such as the UN Firearms Protocol, Stability Pact Regional Implementation Plan, the UN Programme of Action (PoA) and the OSCE Documents on Stockpiles of Conventional Ammunition.
</p><p>Since declaring independence in 1991, Moldova has participated in UN peacekeeping missions in Liberia, Côte d'Ivoire, Sudan and Georgia.
</p><p>Moldova signed a military agreement with Romania to strengthen regional security. The agreement is part of Moldova's strategy to reform its military and cooperate with its neighbours.<sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 12 November 2014, the US donated to Moldovan Armed Forces 39 <a href="/wiki/Humvees" class="mw-redirect" title="Humvees">Humvees</a> and 10 trailers, with a value of US$700,000, to the 22nd Peacekeeping Battalion of the Moldovan National Army to "increase the capability of Moldovan peacekeeping contingents."<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Human_rights">Human rights</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Human_rights_in_Moldova" title="Human rights in Moldova">Human rights in Moldova</a></div>
<p>According to <a href="/wiki/Amnesty_International" title="Amnesty International">Amnesty International</a>, as of 2004 "Torture and other ill-treatment in police detention remained widespread; the state failed to carry out prompt and impartial investigations and police officers sometimes evaded penalties. Political dissidents from <a href="/wiki/Ila%C8%99cu_Group" class="mw-redirect" title="Ilașcu Group">Ilașcu Group</a> were released from arbitrary detention in the break-away Transdinester region only after an order of the <a href="/wiki/European_Court_of_Human_Rights" title="European Court of Human Rights">European Court of Human Rights</a>."<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> In 2009, when Moldova experienced its most <a href="/wiki/April_2009_Moldovan_parliamentary_election_protests" title="April 2009 Moldovan parliamentary election protests">serious civil unrest</a> in a decade, several civilians, including <a href="/wiki/Valeriu_Boboc" class="mw-redirect" title="Valeriu Boboc">Valeriu Boboc</a>, were killed and many more injured.<sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to <a href="/wiki/United_States%27_Country_Reports_on_Human_Rights_Practices" class="mw-redirect" title="United States&#39; Country Reports on Human Rights Practices">Human Rights Report</a> of the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Department_of_State" title="United States Department of State">United States Department of State</a>, released in April 2011, "In contrast to the previous year, there were no reports of killings by security forces. During the year reports of government exercising undue influence over the media substantially decreased." But "Transnistrian authorities continued to harass independent media and opposition lawmakers; restrict freedom of association, movement, and religion; and discriminate against Romanian speakers."<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> Moldova "has made noteworthy progress on religious freedom since the era of the Soviet Union, but it can still take further steps to foster diversity," said the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Special_Rapporteur_on_Freedom_of_Religion_or_Belief" title="United Nations Special Rapporteur on Freedom of Religion or Belief">UN Special Rapporteur on freedom of religion or belief</a> <a href="/wiki/Heiner_Bielefeldt" title="Heiner Bielefeldt">Heiner Bielefeldt</a>, in Chișinău, in September 2011.<sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> Moldova improved its legislation by enacting the <i>Law on Preventing and Combating Family Violence</i>, in 2008.<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_divisions">Administrative divisions</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_Moldova" title="Administrative divisions of Moldova">Administrative divisions of Moldova</a>, <a href="/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Moldova" class="mw-redirect" title="List of cities in Moldova">List of cities in Moldova</a>, <a href="/wiki/List_of_localities_in_Moldova" title="List of localities in Moldova">List of localities in Moldova</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Communes_of_Moldova" title="Communes of Moldova">Communes of Moldova</a></div>

<div style="position:absolute; font-size:85%; left:201.2px; top:311.9px"><i><a href="/wiki/Comrat" title="Comrat"><span style="font-size:110%; color:#000000;">Comrat</span></a></i></div>

<div style="position:absolute; font-size:85%; left:57px; top:216px"><i><a href="/wiki/Rom%C3%A2nia" class="mw-redirect" title="România"><span style="color: grey;">Romania</span></a></i></div>
<div style="position:absolute; font-size:85%; left:274px; top:132px"><i><a href="/wiki/Ukraine" title="Ukraine"><span style="color: grey;">Ukraine</span></a></i></div>
</div>
<p>Moldova is divided into 32 districts (<i>raioane</i>, singular <i><a href="/wiki/Raion" title="Raion">raion</a></i>), three municipalities and two <a href="/wiki/Autonomous_territorial_unit" title="Autonomous territorial unit">autonomous regions</a> (<a href="/wiki/Gagauzia" title="Gagauzia">Gagauzia</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Administrative-Territorial_Units_of_the_Left_Bank_of_the_Dniester" title="Administrative-Territorial Units of the Left Bank of the Dniester">Left Bank of the Dniester</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> The final status of Transnistria is <a href="/wiki/Disputed_status_of_Transnistria" class="mw-redirect" title="Disputed status of Transnistria">disputed</a>, as the central government does not control that territory. 10 other cities, including <a href="/wiki/Comrat" title="Comrat">Comrat</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tiraspol" title="Tiraspol">Tiraspol</a>, the administrative seats of the two autonomous territories, also have <a href="/wiki/Municipiu" title="Municipiu">municipality status</a>.
</p><p>Moldova has 66 cities (towns), including 13 with municipality status, and 916 communes. Another 700 villages are too small to have a separate administration and are administratively part of either cities (41 of them) or communes (659). This makes for a total of 1,682 localities in Moldova, two of which are uninhabited.<sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<p>The largest city in Moldova is Chișinău with a population of 635,994 people. 
</p>
<div style="clear:both;"></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_Moldova" title="Geography of Moldova">Geography of Moldova</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Malul_abrupt_al_Nistrului_Naslavcea-Verejeni_Ocnita_(11).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/Malul_abrupt_al_Nistrului_Naslavcea-Verejeni_Ocnita_%2811%29.jpg/220px-Malul_abrupt_al_Nistrului_Naslavcea-Verejeni_Ocnita_%2811%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/Malul_abrupt_al_Nistrului_Naslavcea-Verejeni_Ocnita_%2811%29.jpg/330px-Malul_abrupt_al_Nistrului_Naslavcea-Verejeni_Ocnita_%2811%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/Malul_abrupt_al_Nistrului_Naslavcea-Verejeni_Ocnita_%2811%29.jpg/440px-Malul_abrupt_al_Nistrului_Naslavcea-Verejeni_Ocnita_%2811%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1440" data-file-height="965" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Malul_abrupt_al_Nistrului_Naslavcea-Verejeni_Ocnita_(11).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Scenery in Moldova, with <a href="/wiki/Dniester_River" class="mw-redirect" title="Dniester River">Dniester River</a></div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Dniester_near_Vadul_lui_Vod%C4%83.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/81/Dniester_near_Vadul_lui_Vod%C4%83.jpg/220px-Dniester_near_Vadul_lui_Vod%C4%83.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/81/Dniester_near_Vadul_lui_Vod%C4%83.jpg/330px-Dniester_near_Vadul_lui_Vod%C4%83.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/81/Dniester_near_Vadul_lui_Vod%C4%83.jpg/440px-Dniester_near_Vadul_lui_Vod%C4%83.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2272" data-file-height="1704" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Dniester_near_Vadul_lui_Vod%C4%83.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Beach on the shore of <a href="/wiki/Dniester_River" class="mw-redirect" title="Dniester River">Dniester River</a> near Vadul lui Vodă</div></div></div>
<p>Moldova lies between latitudes <a href="/wiki/45th_parallel_north" title="45th parallel north">45°</a> and <a href="/wiki/49th_parallel_north" title="49th parallel north">49° N</a>, and mostly between meridians <a href="/wiki/26th_meridian_east" title="26th meridian east">26°</a> and <a href="/wiki/30th_meridian_east" title="30th meridian east">30° E</a> (a small area lies east of 30°). The total land area is 33,851&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (13,070&#160;sq&#160;mi)
</p><p>The largest part of the country (around 88% of the area) lies in <a href="/wiki/Bessarabia" title="Bessarabia">Bessarabia</a> region, between <a href="/wiki/Prut" title="Prut">Prut</a> and <a href="/wiki/Dniester" title="Dniester">Dniester</a> rivers, while a narrow strip in the east is located in <a href="/wiki/Transnistria_(region)" class="mw-redirect" title="Transnistria (region)">Transnistria</a> (east of the Dniester). The western border of Moldova is formed by the Prut river, which joins the <a href="/wiki/Danube" title="Danube">Danube</a> before flowing into the <a href="/wiki/Black_Sea" title="Black Sea">Black Sea</a>. Moldova has access to the Danube for only about 480&#160;m (1,575&#160;ft), and <a href="/wiki/Giurgiule%C8%99ti" title="Giurgiulești">Giurgiulești</a> is the only Moldovan port on the Danube. In the east, the Dniester is the main river, flowing through the country from north to south, receiving the waters of <a href="/wiki/R%C4%83ut" title="Răut">Răut</a>, <a href="/wiki/B%C3%AEc" title="Bîc">Bîc</a>, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Ichel&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Ichel (page does not exist)">Ichel</a>, <a href="/wiki/Botna" title="Botna">Botna</a>. <a href="/wiki/Ialpug" title="Ialpug">Ialpug</a> flows into one of the Danube <a href="/wiki/Liman_(landform)" title="Liman (landform)">limans</a>, while <a href="/wiki/Cog%C3%A2lnic" title="Cogâlnic">Cogâlnic</a> into the Black Sea chain of limans.
</p><p>The country is landlocked, though it is close to the Black Sea; at its closest point it is separated from the <a href="/wiki/Dniester_Liman" class="mw-redirect" title="Dniester Liman">Dniester Liman</a>, an estuary of the Black Sea, by only 3&#160;km of Ukrainian territory. While most of the country is hilly, elevations never exceed 430&#160;m (1,411&#160;ft) – the highest point being the <a href="/wiki/Dealul_B%C4%83l%C4%83ne%C8%99ti" class="mw-redirect" title="Dealul Bălănești">Bălănești Hill</a>. Moldova's hills are part of the Moldavian Plateau, which geologically originate from the <a href="/wiki/Carpathian_Mountains" title="Carpathian Mountains">Carpathian Mountains</a>. Its subdivisions in Moldova include the Dniester Hills (Northern Moldavian Hills and <a href="/w/index.php?title=Dniester-R%C4%81ut_Ridge&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Dniester-Rāut Ridge (page does not exist)">Dniester Ridge</a>), the Moldavian Plain (Middle Prut Valley and <a href="/wiki/B%C4%83l%C8%9Bi_Steppe" title="Bălți Steppe">Bălți Steppe</a>), and the Central Moldavian Plateau (<a href="/w/index.php?title=Ciuluc-Solone%C8%9B_Hills&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Ciuluc-Soloneț Hills (page does not exist)">Ciuluc-Soloneț Hills</a>, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Corne%C8%99ti_Hills&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Cornești Hills (page does not exist)">Cornești Hills</a>—<a href="/wiki/Codri" class="mw-redirect" title="Codri">Codri</a> Massive, "Codri" meaning "forests"—<a href="/w/index.php?title=Lower_Dniester_Hills&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Lower Dniester Hills (page does not exist)">Lower Dniester Hills</a>, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Lower_Prut_Valley&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Lower Prut Valley (page does not exist)">Lower Prut Valley</a>, and <a href="/w/index.php?title=Tigheci_Hills&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Tigheci Hills (page does not exist)">Tigheci Hills</a>). In the south, the country has a small flatland, the <a href="/wiki/Bugeac_Plain" class="mw-redirect" title="Bugeac Plain">Bugeac Plain</a>. The territory of Moldova east of the river Dniester is split between parts of the <a href="/wiki/Podolian_Plateau" class="mw-redirect" title="Podolian Plateau">Podolian Plateau</a>, and parts of the <a href="/wiki/Eurasian_Steppe" title="Eurasian Steppe">Eurasian Steppe</a>.
</p><p>The country's main cities are the capital Chișinău, in the centre of the country, Tiraspol (in the eastern region of Transnistria), Bălți (in the north) and Bender (in the south-east). <a href="/wiki/Comrat" title="Comrat">Comrat</a> is the administrative centre of Gagauzia.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Orhei_Vechi,_Moldova_-_Flickr_-_Dave_Proffer_(13).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/Orhei_Vechi%2C_Moldova_-_Flickr_-_Dave_Proffer_%2813%29.jpg/220px-Orhei_Vechi%2C_Moldova_-_Flickr_-_Dave_Proffer_%2813%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/Orhei_Vechi%2C_Moldova_-_Flickr_-_Dave_Proffer_%2813%29.jpg/330px-Orhei_Vechi%2C_Moldova_-_Flickr_-_Dave_Proffer_%2813%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/Orhei_Vechi%2C_Moldova_-_Flickr_-_Dave_Proffer_%2813%29.jpg/440px-Orhei_Vechi%2C_Moldova_-_Flickr_-_Dave_Proffer_%2813%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3264" data-file-height="2448" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Orhei_Vechi,_Moldova_-_Flickr_-_Dave_Proffer_(13).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Cave churches at <a href="/wiki/Old_Orhei" title="Old Orhei">Old Orhei</a>, part of the <a href="/wiki/Protected_areas_of_Moldova" title="Protected areas of Moldova">only national park</a> in the country</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Toltre_FT%C8%98T-52.JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/Toltre_FT%C8%98T-52.JPG/220px-Toltre_FT%C8%98T-52.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/Toltre_FT%C8%98T-52.JPG/330px-Toltre_FT%C8%98T-52.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/Toltre_FT%C8%98T-52.JPG/440px-Toltre_FT%C8%98T-52.JPG 2x" data-file-width="4320" data-file-height="3240" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Toltre_FT%C8%98T-52.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Toltrele Prutului near <a href="/wiki/Fete%C8%99ti,_Edine%C8%9B" title="Fetești, Edineț">Fetești</a>, <a href="/wiki/Edine%C8%9B_District" title="Edineț District">Edineț District</a></div></div></div>
<p>Moldova has a climate which is moderately continental; its proximity to the <a href="/wiki/Black_Sea" title="Black Sea">Black Sea</a> leads to the climate being mildly cold in the autumn and winter and relatively cool in the spring and summer.<sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The summers are warm and long, with temperatures averaging about 20&#160;°C (68&#160;°F) and the winters are relatively mild and dry, with January temperatures averaging −4&#160;°C (25&#160;°F). Annual rainfall, which ranges from around 600&#160;mm (24&#160;in) in the north to 400&#160;mm (16&#160;in) in the south, can vary greatly; long dry spells are not unusual. The heaviest rainfall occurs in early summer and again in October; heavy showers and thunderstorms are common. Because of the irregular terrain, heavy summer rains often cause erosion and river silting.
</p><p>The highest temperature ever recorded in Moldova was 41.5&#160;°C (106.7&#160;°F) on 21 July 2007 in <a href="/wiki/Camenca" title="Camenca">Camenca</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> The lowest temperature ever recorded was −35.5&#160;°C (−31.9&#160;°F) on 20 January 1963 in Brătușeni, Edineț county.<sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Biodiversity">Biodiversity</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Protected_areas_in_Moldova" class="mw-redirect" title="Protected areas in Moldova">Protected areas in Moldova</a></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Phytogeography" title="Phytogeography">Phytogeographically</a>, Moldova is split between the <a href="/wiki/East_European_Plain" title="East European Plain">East European Plain</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Pontic%E2%80%93Caspian_steppe" title="Pontic–Caspian steppe">Pontic–Caspian steppe</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Circumboreal_Region" title="Circumboreal Region">Circumboreal Region</a> within the <a href="/wiki/Boreal_Kingdom" title="Boreal Kingdom">Boreal Kingdom</a>. It is home to three terrestrial ecoregions: <a href="/wiki/Central_European_mixed_forests" title="Central European mixed forests">Central European mixed forests</a>, <a href="/wiki/East_European_forest_steppe" title="East European forest steppe">East European forest steppe</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Pontic_steppe" class="mw-redirect" title="Pontic steppe">Pontic steppe</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-DinersteinOlson2017_148-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-148">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup> Forests currently cover only 11% of Moldova, though the state is making efforts to increase their range. It had a 2019 <a href="/wiki/Forest_Landscape_Integrity_Index" title="Forest Landscape Integrity Index">Forest Landscape Integrity Index</a> mean score of 2.2/10, ranking it 158th globally out of 172 countries.<sup id="cite_ref-FLII-Supplementary_149-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FLII-Supplementary-149">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> Game animals, such as <a href="/wiki/Red_deer" title="Red deer">red deer</a>, <a href="/wiki/Roe_deer" title="Roe deer">roe deer</a> and <a href="/wiki/Wild_boar" title="Wild boar">wild boar</a> can be found in these wooded areas.<sup id="cite_ref-Environmental_issues_in_Moldova_150-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Environmental_issues_in_Moldova-150">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup></p><div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Saiga_antelope_at_the_Stepnoi_Sanctuary.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/57/Saiga_antelope_at_the_Stepnoi_Sanctuary.jpg/220px-Saiga_antelope_at_the_Stepnoi_Sanctuary.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="186" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/57/Saiga_antelope_at_the_Stepnoi_Sanctuary.jpg/330px-Saiga_antelope_at_the_Stepnoi_Sanctuary.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/57/Saiga_antelope_at_the_Stepnoi_Sanctuary.jpg/440px-Saiga_antelope_at_the_Stepnoi_Sanctuary.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2449" data-file-height="2076" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Saiga_antelope_at_the_Stepnoi_Sanctuary.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Noted for its vivid portrayal of the lower <a href="/wiki/Dniester_river" class="mw-redirect" title="Dniester river">Dniester river</a>, <a href="/wiki/Henryk_Sienkiewicz" title="Henryk Sienkiewicz">Henryk Sienkiewicz</a>'s novel <i><a href="/wiki/With_Fire_and_Sword" title="With Fire and Sword">With Fire and Sword</a></i> opens with a description of <a href="/wiki/Saiga_antelope" title="Saiga antelope">saigas</a> as a way to highlight the story's exotic setting.<sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup> Saigas are a <a href="/wiki/Critically_endangered" class="mw-redirect" title="Critically endangered">critically endangered</a> <a href="/wiki/Species" title="Species">species</a> that is now extinct in Moldova.</div></div></div>

<p>The environment of Moldova suffered extreme degradation during the Soviet period, when industrial and agricultural development proceeded without regard for environmental protection.<sup id="cite_ref-Environmental_issues_in_Moldova_150-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Environmental_issues_in_Moldova-150">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup> Excessive use of pesticides resulted in heavily polluted topsoil, and industries lacked emission controls.<sup id="cite_ref-Environmental_issues_in_Moldova_150-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Environmental_issues_in_Moldova-150">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup> Founded in 1990, the <a href="/wiki/Ecological_Movement_of_Moldova" title="Ecological Movement of Moldova">Ecological Movement of Moldova</a>, a national, non-governmental, nonprofit organization which is a member of the <a href="/wiki/International_Union_for_Conservation_of_Nature" title="International Union for Conservation of Nature">International Union for Conservation of Nature</a> has been working to restore Moldova's damaged natural environment.<sup id="cite_ref-Environmental_issues_in_Moldova_150-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Environmental_issues_in_Moldova-150">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup> The movement is national representative of the Center "Naturopa" of the <a href="/wiki/Council_of_Europe" title="Council of Europe">Council of Europe</a> and <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Environment_Programme" title="United Nations Environment Programme">United Nations Environment Programme</a> of the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Once possessing a range from the <a href="/wiki/British_Isles" title="British Isles">British Isles</a> through <a href="/wiki/Central_Asia" title="Central Asia">Central Asia</a> over the <a href="/wiki/Bering_Strait" title="Bering Strait">Bering Strait</a> into <a href="/wiki/Alaska" title="Alaska">Alaska</a> and Canada's <a href="/wiki/Yukon" title="Yukon">Yukon</a> as well as the <a href="/wiki/Northwest_Territories" title="Northwest Territories">Northwest Territories</a>, <a href="/wiki/Saiga_antelope" title="Saiga antelope">saigas</a> survived in Moldova and Romania into the late 18th century. Deforestation, demographic pressure, as well as excessive hunting eradicated the native saiga herds which is <a href="/wiki/Critically_endangered" class="mw-redirect" title="Critically endangered">currently threatened with extinction</a>. They were considered a characteristic animal of <a href="/wiki/Scythia" title="Scythia">Scythia</a> in antiquity. Historian <a href="/wiki/Strabo" title="Strabo">Strabo</a> referred to the saigas as the <i>kolos</i>, describing it as "between the deer and ram in size" which (understandably but wrongly) was believed to drink through its nose.<sup id="cite_ref-154" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-154">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Another animal which was extinct in Moldova since the 18th century until recently was the European Wood Bison or <a href="/wiki/Wisent" class="mw-redirect" title="Wisent">wisent</a>. The species was reintroduced with the arrival of three European bison from <a href="/wiki/Bia%C5%82owie%C5%BCa_Forest" title="Białowieża Forest">Białowieża Forest</a> in Poland several days before <a href="/wiki/Independence_Day_of_the_Republic_of_Moldova" title="Independence Day of the Republic of Moldova">Moldova's Independence Day</a> on 27 August 2005.<sup id="cite_ref-155" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-155">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup> Moldova is currently interested in expanding their wisent population, and began talks with Belarus in 2019 regarding a bison exchange program between the two countries.<sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Moldova" title="Economy of Moldova">Economy of Moldova</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Tourism_in_Moldova" title="Tourism in Moldova">Tourism in Moldova</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1097763485"/><table class="box-Overly_detailed plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-style ambox-overly_detailed" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/f/f2/Edit-clear.svg/40px-Edit-clear.svg.png" decoding="async" width="40" height="40" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/f/f2/Edit-clear.svg/60px-Edit-clear.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/f/f2/Edit-clear.svg/80px-Edit-clear.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="48" data-file-height="48" /></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section <b>may contain an excessive amount of intricate detail that may interest only a particular audience</b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help by <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Content_forking#Article_spinoffs:_.22Summary_style.22_meta-articles_and_summary_sections" title="Wikipedia:Content forking">spinning off</a> or <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Handling_trivia#Recommendations_for_handling_trivia" title="Wikipedia:Handling trivia">relocating</a> any relevant information, and removing excessive detail that may be against <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:What_Wikipedia_is_not" title="Wikipedia:What Wikipedia is not">Wikipedia's inclusion policy</a>.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">October 2016</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this template message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:262px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Moldova_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ca/Moldova_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/260px-Moldova_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="260" height="169" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ca/Moldova_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/390px-Moldova_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ca/Moldova_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/520px-Moldova_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Moldova_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A proportional representation of Moldova exports, 2019</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:MoldovanGDPBySector.svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/MoldovanGDPBySector.svg/220px-MoldovanGDPBySector.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="264" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/MoldovanGDPBySector.svg/330px-MoldovanGDPBySector.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/MoldovanGDPBySector.svg/440px-MoldovanGDPBySector.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1250" data-file-height="1500" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:MoldovanGDPBySector.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Moldova GDP by sector</div></div></div>
<p>After the breakup of the USSR in 1991, energy shortages, political uncertainty, trade obstacles and weak administrative capacity contributed to the decline of Moldova's economy. As a part of an ambitious economic liberalization effort, Moldova introduced a convertible currency, liberalized all prices, stopped issuing preferential credits to state enterprises, backed steady land privatization, removed export controls, and liberalized interest rates. The government entered into agreements with the World Bank and the International Monetary Fund to promote growth. 
The economy subsequently declined from 1991 to 1999. Since 2000, however, the country's GDP (PPP) grew significantly:<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook_Moldova_21-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook_Moldova-21">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<p>Although estimates point to possible modest overvaluation of the real exchange rate, external competitiveness appears broadly adequate as reflected in strong sustained export performance.<sup id="cite_ref-imf.org_158-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-imf.org-158">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup> However, the near-term economic outlook is weak. Main risks to the near-term outlook relate to serious vulnerabilities and governance issues in the banking sector, policy slippages in the run up to the elections, intensification of geopolitical tensions in the region, and a further slowdown in activity in main trading partners.
</p><p>Moldova remains highly vulnerable to fluctuations in remittances from workers abroad (which constitute 24 percent of GDP), exports to the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Independent_States" title="Commonwealth of Independent States">Commonwealth of Independent States</a> (CIS) and <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a> (EU) (88 per cent of total exports), and donor support (about 10 per cent of government spending). The main transmission channels through which adverse exogenous shocks could impact the Moldovan economy are remittances (also due to potentially returning migrants), external trade, and capital flows.
</p><p>Moldova largely achieved the main objectives of the combined ECF/EFF (IMF financial credit) supported program. The economy recovered from the drought-related contraction in 2012.
</p>

<p>The gross average monthly salary in the Republic of Moldova has registered a steady positive growth after 1999, being 5906 lei or 298 euros in 2018.
</p><p>Corporate governance in the banking sector is a major concern. In line with FSAP recommendations, significant weaknesses in the legal and regulatory frameworks must be urgently addressed to ensure stability and soundness of the financial sector. Moldova has achieved a substantial degree of fiscal consolidation in recent years, but this trend is now reversing. Resisting pre-election pressures for selective spending increases and returning to the path of fiscal consolidation would reduce reliance on exceptionally high donor support. Structural fiscal reforms would help safeguard sustainability.<sup id="cite_ref-imf.org_158-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-imf.org-158">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup> Monetary policy has been successful in maintaining inflation within the NBM's target range. The implementation of structural reforms outlined in the National Development Strategy (NDS) Moldova 2020—especially in the business environment, physical infrastructure, and human resources development areas—would help boost potential growth and reduce poverty.<sup id="cite_ref-imf.org_158-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-imf.org-158">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup> Moldova's remarkable recovery from the severe recession of 2009 was largely the result of sound macroeconomic and financial policies and structural reforms. Despite a small contraction in 2012, Moldova's economic performance was among the strongest in the region during 2010–13. Economic activity grew cumulatively by about 24 percent; consumer price inflation was brought under control; and real wages increased cumulatively by about 13 percent. This expansion was made possible by adequate macroeconomic stabilization measures and ambitious structural reforms implemented in the wake of the crisis under a Fund-supported program. In November 2013, Moldova initialed an Association Agreement with the EU which includes provisions establishing a <a href="/wiki/Deep_and_Comprehensive_Free_Trade_Area" title="Deep and Comprehensive Free Trade Area">Deep and Comprehensive Free Trade Area</a> (DCFTA).
</p>
