<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:GDP_per_capita_development_of_Moldova.svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/GDP_per_capita_development_of_Moldova.svg/220px-GDP_per_capita_development_of_Moldova.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="155" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/GDP_per_capita_development_of_Moldova.svg/330px-GDP_per_capita_development_of_Moldova.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/GDP_per_capita_development_of_Moldova.svg/440px-GDP_per_capita_development_of_Moldova.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="850" data-file-height="600" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:GDP_per_capita_development_of_Moldova.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Real GPD per capita development of Moldova, 1973 to 2018</div></div></div>
<p>A political crisis in early 2013 led to policy slippages in the fiscal and financial areas. The political crisis that broke out in early 2013 was resolved with the appointment of a government supported by a pro-European center-right/center coalition in May 2013. However, delays in policy implementation prevented completion of the final reviews under the ECF/EFF arrangements.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2016)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Endava_Tower,_%D0%9A%D0%B8%D1%88%D0%B8%D0%BD%D0%B5%D0%B2,_%D0%A0%D0%B5%D1%81%D0%BF%D1%83%D0%B1%D0%BB%D0%B8%D0%BA%D0%B0_%D0%9C%D0%BE%D0%BB%D0%B4%D0%BE%D0%B2%D0%B0_Endava_Tower,_Chisinau,_Republic_Moldova_Endava_Tower,_Chisinau,_Republic_of_Moldova_(51191814936).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/23/Endava_Tower%2C_%D0%9A%D0%B8%D1%88%D0%B8%D0%BD%D0%B5%D0%B2%2C_%D0%A0%D0%B5%D1%81%D0%BF%D1%83%D0%B1%D0%BB%D0%B8%D0%BA%D0%B0_%D0%9C%D0%BE%D0%BB%D0%B4%D0%BE%D0%B2%D0%B0_Endava_Tower%2C_Chisinau%2C_Republic_Moldova_Endava_Tower%2C_Chisinau%2C_Republic_of_Moldova_%2851191814936%29.jpg/220px-thumbnail.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/23/Endava_Tower%2C_%D0%9A%D0%B8%D1%88%D0%B8%D0%BD%D0%B5%D0%B2%2C_%D0%A0%D0%B5%D1%81%D0%BF%D1%83%D0%B1%D0%BB%D0%B8%D0%BA%D0%B0_%D0%9C%D0%BE%D0%BB%D0%B4%D0%BE%D0%B2%D0%B0_Endava_Tower%2C_Chisinau%2C_Republic_Moldova_Endava_Tower%2C_Chisinau%2C_Republic_of_Moldova_%2851191814936%29.jpg/330px-thumbnail.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/23/Endava_Tower%2C_%D0%9A%D0%B8%D1%88%D0%B8%D0%BD%D0%B5%D0%B2%2C_%D0%A0%D0%B5%D1%81%D0%BF%D1%83%D0%B1%D0%BB%D0%B8%D0%BA%D0%B0_%D0%9C%D0%BE%D0%BB%D0%B4%D0%BE%D0%B2%D0%B0_Endava_Tower%2C_Chisinau%2C_Republic_Moldova_Endava_Tower%2C_Chisinau%2C_Republic_of_Moldova_%2851191814936%29.jpg/440px-thumbnail.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4839" data-file-height="3227" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Endava_Tower,_%D0%9A%D0%B8%D1%88%D0%B8%D0%BD%D0%B5%D0%B2,_%D0%A0%D0%B5%D1%81%D0%BF%D1%83%D0%B1%D0%BB%D0%B8%D0%BA%D0%B0_%D0%9C%D0%BE%D0%BB%D0%B4%D0%BE%D0%B2%D0%B0_Endava_Tower,_Chisinau,_Republic_Moldova_Endava_Tower,_Chisinau,_Republic_of_Moldova_(51191814936).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Malldova" class="mw-redirect" title="Malldova">MallDova</a> shopping centre in Chișinău</div></div></div>
<p>Despite a sharp decline in poverty in recent years, Moldova remains one of the poorest countries in Europe and structural reforms are needed to promote sustainable growth. Based on the Europe and Central Asia (ECA) regional poverty line of US$5/day (PPP), 55 percent of the population was poor in 2011. While this was significantly lower than 94 percent in 2002, Moldova's poverty rate is still more than double the ECA average of 25 percent. The NDS—Moldova (National Development System) 2020, which was published in November 2012, focuses on several critical areas to boost economic development and reduce poverty. These include education, infrastructure, financial sector, business climate, energy consumption, pension system, and judicial framework. Following the <a href="/wiki/1998_Russian_financial_crisis" title="1998 Russian financial crisis">regional financial crisis in 1998</a>, Moldova has made significant progress towards achieving and retaining macroeconomic and financial stabilization. It has, furthermore, implemented many structural and institutional reforms that are indispensable for the efficient functioning of a market economy. These efforts have helped maintain macroeconomic and financial stability under difficult external circumstances, enabled the resumption of economic growth and contributed to establishing an environment conducive to the economy's further growth and development in the medium term.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2016)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The government's goal of <a href="/wiki/Moldova%E2%80%93European_Union_relations" title="Moldova–European Union relations">EU integration</a> has resulted in some market-oriented progress. Moldova experienced better than expected economic growth in 2013 due to increased agriculture production, to economic policies adopted by the Moldovan government since 2009, and to the receipt of EU trade preferences connecting Moldovan products to the world's largest market. Moldova has signed the <a href="/wiki/Moldova%E2%80%93European_Union_Association_Agreement" title="Moldova–European Union Association Agreement">Association Agreement</a> and the Deep and Comprehensive Free Trade Agreement with the European Union during summer 2014.<sup id="cite_ref-169" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-169">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup> Moldova has also achieved a Free Visa Regime<sup id="cite_ref-170" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-170">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup> with the EU which represents the biggest achievement of Moldovan diplomacy since independence.<sup id="cite_ref-171" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-171">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup> Still, growth has been hampered by high prices for Russian natural gas, a Russian import ban on Moldovan wine, increased foreign scrutiny of Moldovan agricultural products, and by Moldova's large external debt. Over the longer term, Moldova's economy remains vulnerable to political uncertainty, weak administrative capacity, vested bureaucratic interests, corruption, higher fuel prices, Russian pressure, and the separatist regime in Moldova's Transnistria region.<sup id="cite_ref-indexmundi.com_172-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-indexmundi.com-172">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup>
According to IMF World Economic Outlook April 2014, the Moldovan GDP (PPP) per capita is 3,927 International Dollars,<sup id="cite_ref-173" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-173">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-174" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-174">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup> excluding grey economy and tax evasion.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Energy">Energy</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Energy_in_Moldova" title="Energy in Moldova">Energy in Moldova</a></div>
<p>With few natural energy resources, Moldova imports almost all of its energy supplies from Russia and Ukraine. Moldova's dependence on Russian energy is underscored by a growing US$5 billion debt to Russian natural gas supplier Gazprom, largely the result of unreimbursed natural gas consumption in the separatist Transnistria region. In August 2013, work began on a new pipeline between Moldova and Romania that may eventually break Russia's monopoly on Moldova's gas supplies.<sup id="cite_ref-indexmundi.com_172-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-indexmundi.com-172">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> Moldova is a partner country of the EU <a href="/wiki/INOGATE" title="INOGATE">INOGATE</a> energy programme, which has four key topics: enhancing <a href="/wiki/Energy_security" title="Energy security">energy security</a>, <a href="/wiki/Technological_convergence" title="Technological convergence">convergence</a> of member state <a href="/wiki/Energy_market" title="Energy market">energy markets</a> on the basis of <a href="/wiki/Internal_energy_market" class="mw-redirect" title="Internal energy market">EU internal energy market</a> principles, supporting <a href="/wiki/Sustainable_energy" title="Sustainable energy">sustainable energy</a> development, and attracting investment for energy projects of common and regional interest.<sup id="cite_ref-175" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-175">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Wine_industry">Wine industry</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Moldovan_wine" title="Moldovan wine">Moldovan wine</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Mile%C5%9Ftii_Mici_(3944427747).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/Mile%C5%9Ftii_Mici_%283944427747%29.jpg/220px-Mile%C5%9Ftii_Mici_%283944427747%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/Mile%C5%9Ftii_Mici_%283944427747%29.jpg/330px-Mile%C5%9Ftii_Mici_%283944427747%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/Mile%C5%9Ftii_Mici_%283944427747%29.jpg/440px-Mile%C5%9Ftii_Mici_%283944427747%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3264" data-file-height="2448" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Mile%C5%9Ftii_Mici_(3944427747).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Mile%C8%99tii_Mici" title="Mileștii Mici">Mileștii Mici</a> is home to the world's biggest <a href="/wiki/Wine_cellars" class="mw-redirect" title="Wine cellars">wine cellar</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>The country has a well-established <a href="/wiki/Wine" title="Wine">wine</a> industry. It has a vineyard area of 147,000 hectares (360,000 acres), of which 102,500&#160;ha (253,000 acres) are used for commercial production. Most of the country's wine production is made for export. Many families have their own recipes and <a href="/wiki/Grape_varieties" class="mw-redirect" title="Grape varieties">grape varieties</a> that have been passed down through the generations. There are 3 historical wine regions: Valul lui Traian (south west), Stefan Voda (south east) and <a href="/wiki/Codru_(wine)" title="Codru (wine)">Codru</a> (center), destined for the production of wines with protected geographic indication.<sup id="cite_ref-natura2000_22-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-natura2000-22">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Mile%C8%99tii_Mici_(winery)" title="Mileștii Mici (winery)">Mileștii Mici</a> is the home of the largest wine cellar in the world. It stretches for 200&#160;km (120&#160;mi) (though only 55&#160;km (34&#160;mi) is in use) and holds almost 2 million bottles of wine<sup id="cite_ref-176" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-176">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Agriculture">Agriculture</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Agriculture_in_Moldova" title="Agriculture in Moldova">Agriculture in Moldova</a></div>
<p>Moldova's <a href="/wiki/Chernozem" title="Chernozem">rich soil</a> and <a href="/wiki/Temperate" class="mw-redirect" title="Temperate">temperate</a> <a href="/wiki/Continental_climate" title="Continental climate">continental climate</a> (with warm summers and mild winters) have made the country one of the most productive agricultural regions since ancient times, and a major supplier of agricultural products in southeastern Europe. In agriculture, the economic reform started with the land cadastre reform.<sup id="cite_ref-cadastre_law_177-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cadastre_law-177">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup>
Moldova's agricultural products include vegetables, fruits, grapes, <a href="/wiki/Wine" title="Wine">wine</a>, and grains.<sup id="cite_ref-178" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-178">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Transport">Transport</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Transport_in_Moldova" title="Transport in Moldova">Transport in Moldova</a> and <a href="/wiki/Rail_transport_in_Moldova" class="mw-redirect" title="Rail transport in Moldova">Rail transport in Moldova</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:19-01-19-Chi%C8%99in%C4%83u_RRK0176.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/19-01-19-Chi%C8%99in%C4%83u_RRK0176.jpg/220px-19-01-19-Chi%C8%99in%C4%83u_RRK0176.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/19-01-19-Chi%C8%99in%C4%83u_RRK0176.jpg/330px-19-01-19-Chi%C8%99in%C4%83u_RRK0176.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/19-01-19-Chi%C8%99in%C4%83u_RRK0176.jpg/440px-19-01-19-Chi%C8%99in%C4%83u_RRK0176.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4288" data-file-height="2848" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:19-01-19-Chi%C8%99in%C4%83u_RRK0176.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Chi%C8%99in%C4%83u_International_Airport" title="Chișinău International Airport">Chișinău International Airport</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>The main means of transportation in Moldova are railways 1,138&#160;km (707&#160;mi) and a highway system (12,730&#160;km or 7,910&#160;mi overall, including 10,937&#160;km or 6,796&#160;mi of paved surfaces). The sole international air gateway of Moldova is the <a href="/wiki/Chi%C8%99in%C4%83u_International_Airport" title="Chișinău International Airport">Chișinău International Airport</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Giurgiule%C8%99ti" title="Giurgiulești">Giurgiulești</a> terminal on the <a href="/wiki/Danube" title="Danube">Danube</a> is compatible with small seagoing vessels. Shipping on the lower <a href="/wiki/Prut" title="Prut">Prut</a> and <a href="/wiki/Nistru" class="mw-redirect" title="Nistru">Nistru</a> rivers plays only a modest role in the country's transportation system.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Telecommunications">Telecommunications</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Telecommunications_in_Moldova" title="Telecommunications in Moldova">Telecommunications in Moldova</a></div>
<p>The first million mobile telephone users were registered in September 2005. The number of mobile telephone users in Moldova increased by 47.3% in the first quarter of 2008 against the last year and exceeded 2.89 million.<sup id="cite_ref-2mil_179-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2mil-179">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In September 2009, Moldova was the first country in the world to launch high-definition voice services (<a href="/wiki/HD_voice" class="mw-redirect" title="HD voice">HD voice</a>) for mobile phones, and the first country in Europe to launch 14.4 Mbit/s mobile broadband on a national scale, with over 40% population coverage.<sup id="cite_ref-180" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-180">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As of 2010<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Moldova&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, there are around 1,295,000 <a href="/wiki/Internet_in_Moldova" title="Internet in Moldova">Internet users in Moldova</a> with overall Internet penetration of 35.9%.<sup id="cite_ref-181" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-181">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 6 June 2012, the Government approved the licensing of 4G / LTE for mobile operators.<sup id="cite_ref-182" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-182">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1097763485"/><table class="box-Too_many_charts plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-style" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f8/X-office-spreadsheet.svg/40px-X-office-spreadsheet.svg.png" decoding="async" width="40" height="40" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f8/X-office-spreadsheet.svg/60px-X-office-spreadsheet.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f8/X-office-spreadsheet.svg/80px-X-office-spreadsheet.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="48" data-file-height="48" /></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This article contains <b><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTSTATS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTSTATS">too many charts, tables or uncontextualized data</a></b> for its overall length. <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:INDISCRIMINATE" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:INDISCRIMINATE">Stand alone statistics</a> lacking context or explanation can reduce readability and may be confusing.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Relevant discussion may be found on the <a href="/wiki/Talk:Moldova##" title="Talk:Moldova">talk page</a>. <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Moldova" title="Demographics of Moldova">Demographics of Moldova</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Ethnic_composition">Ethnic composition</span></h3>
<p>As of the 2014 census, <a href="/wiki/Moldovans" title="Moldovans">Moldovans</a> were the largest ethnic group of Moldova (75.1% of the population). In addition, 7.0% of the population declared themselves <a href="/wiki/Romanians" title="Romanians">Romanians</a>, amid the <a href="/wiki/Controversy_over_ethnic_and_linguistic_identity_in_Moldova" title="Controversy over ethnic and linguistic identity in Moldova">controversy over ethnic and linguistic identity in Moldova</a>. Although historical, the polarization based on ethnolinguistic criteria of the majority ethnic group reappeared with the national revival movement of the late 1980s, and, so far, there is no consensus regarding the mainstream identity in the Republic of Moldova (Moldovan or Romanian).<sup id="cite_ref-183" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-183">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-184" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-184">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The country also has important minority ethnic communities, as shown in the table below. <a href="/wiki/Gagauz_people_in_Moldova" title="Gagauz people in Moldova">Gagauz</a>, 4.4% of the population, are <a href="/wiki/Christians" title="Christians">Christian</a> <a href="/wiki/Turkic_peoples" title="Turkic peoples">Turkic</a> people. <a href="/wiki/Greeks_in_Moldova" title="Greeks in Moldova">Greeks</a>, <a href="/wiki/Armenians_in_Moldova" title="Armenians in Moldova">Armenians</a>, <a href="/wiki/Poles_in_Moldova" title="Poles in Moldova">Poles</a>, Ukrainians, although not numerous, were present as early as the 17th century, and have contributed cultural marks. The 19th century saw the arrival of many more Ukrainians from <a href="/wiki/Podolia" title="Podolia">Podolia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Galicia_(Central_Europe)" class="mw-redirect" title="Galicia (Central Europe)">Galicia</a>, as well as new communities, such as <a href="/wiki/Lipovans" title="Lipovans">Lipovans</a>, <a href="/wiki/Russians_in_Moldova" title="Russians in Moldova">Russians</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bessarabian_Bulgarians" title="Bessarabian Bulgarians">Bulgarians</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Bessarabia_Germans" title="Bessarabia Germans">Germans</a>. Most of Moldova's <a href="/wiki/Bessarabian_Jews" class="mw-redirect" title="Bessarabian Jews">Jewish population</a> emigrated between 1979 and 2004.
</p>

<tr>
<td colspan="13" align="center"><b>*</b> There is an <a href="/wiki/Controversy_over_ethnic_and_linguistic_identity_in_Moldova" title="Controversy over ethnic and linguistic identity in Moldova">ongoing controversy</a>, in part involving linguisitic definition of ethnicity, over whether Moldovans' self-identification constitutes an ethnic group distinct and apart from Romanians, or a subset.
</td></tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="13" align="center"><b>**</b> There were numerous allegations that the ethnic affiliation numbers were rigged: 7 out of 10 observer groups of the Council of Europe reported a significant number of cases where census-takers recommended respondents declare themselves Moldovans rather than Romanians. Complicating the interpretation of the results, 18.8% of respondents that identified themselves as Moldovans declared Romanian to be their native language.<sup id="cite_ref-191" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-191">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup>
</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>According to the <a href="/wiki/Moldovan_Census_(2014)" class="mw-redirect" title="Moldovan Census (2014)">2014 census</a> preliminary data, 2,998,235 inhabitants lived in Moldova (within the areas controlled by the central government), an 11.3% decrease from the figure recorded at the 2004 census. It is estimated that, as of 2022, 43.2% of the total population live in urban areas, and that the <a href="/wiki/Urbanization_rate" class="mw-redirect" title="Urbanization rate">urbanization rate</a> is 0.09%.<sup id="cite_ref-192" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-192">&#91;187&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:292px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Etnie.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/Etnie.jpg/290px-Etnie.jpg" decoding="async" width="290" height="410" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/Etnie.jpg/435px-Etnie.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/Etnie.jpg/580px-Etnie.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1608" data-file-height="2272" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Etnie.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Ethnic map of the Republic of Moldova (2014)</div></div></div>
<p>According to the last census in Transnistria (October 2015), the population of the region was 475,373, a 14.47% decrease from the figure recorded at the 2004 census. The <a href="/wiki/Urbanization" title="Urbanization">urbanization</a> rate was 69.9%. By ethnic composition, the population of Transnistria was distributed as follows: Russians - 29.1%, Moldovans - 28.6%, Ukrainians - 22.9%, Bulgarians - 2.4%, Gagauzians - 1.1%, Belarusians - 0.5%, Transnistrian - 0.2%, other nationalities - 1.4%. About 14% of the population did not declare their nationality. Also, for the first time, the population had the option to identify as "Transnistrian".<sup id="cite_ref-193" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-193">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Moldova" title="Languages of Moldova">Languages of Moldova</a>, <a href="/wiki/Moldovan_language" title="Moldovan language">Moldovan language</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Romanian_language" title="Romanian language">Romanian language</a></div>
<p>The official language of Moldova is <a href="/wiki/Romanian_language" title="Romanian language">Romanian</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Romance_languages" title="Romance languages">Romance language</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-196" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-196">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The 1991 <a href="/wiki/Moldovan_Declaration_of_Independence" title="Moldovan Declaration of Independence">Declaration of Independence</a> names the official language Romanian.<sup id="cite_ref-197" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-197">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-198" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-198">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Moldova_(1994)" title="Constitution of Moldova (1994)">Constitution of 1994</a> stated that the national language of the Republic of Moldova was <a href="/wiki/Moldovan_language" title="Moldovan language">Moldovan</a>, and its writing is based on the <a href="/wiki/Latin_alphabet" title="Latin alphabet">Latin alphabet</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Constitution_199-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Constitution-199">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2013, the Constitutional Court of Moldova ruled that the name "Romanian", as used in the Declaration of Independence to identify the official language, prevails over the name "Moldovan", given in Article 13 of the Constitution.<sup id="cite_ref-200" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-200">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

</th></tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="7" align="center">* <i>Moldovan language</i> is one of the names used in the Republic of Moldova for the <i>Romanian language</i>.
</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>At the <a href="/wiki/Moldovan_Census_(2014)" class="mw-redirect" title="Moldovan Census (2014)">2014 census</a> (which did not include data from the <a href="/wiki/Transnistria" title="Transnistria">Transnistrian region</a>), 54.7% of the population named Moldovan whereas 24.0% named Romanian as their first language in daily use. Although only 4.1% are ethnic Russians, Russian is still used as the main language by 14.5% of the total population. Around 50% of ethnic Ukrainians, 33% of Gagauz, 33% of Bulgarians, and 5.7% of Moldovans declared Russian as their daily use language.
</p>

<p>Historically Russian was taught in schools as the first foreign language, because of the relationship with the Russian Empire and Soviet Union. In the 21st century, the primary foreign language taught in the schools is English. In 2013 more than 60% of schoolchildren took it as their first foreign language. This was followed by <a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French</a>, taken by less than 50% of students. Since 1996, the Republic of Moldova has been a full member of <a href="/wiki/Organisation_internationale_de_la_Francophonie" title="Organisation internationale de la Francophonie">La Francophonie</a>. <a href="/wiki/German_language" title="German language">German</a> was the third-ranked choice.<sup id="cite_ref-204" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-204">&#91;199&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Moldova" title="Religion in Moldova">Religion in Moldova</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Condri%C8%9Ba_monastery,_Moldova.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/Condri%C8%9Ba_monastery%2C_Moldova.jpg/220px-Condri%C8%9Ba_monastery%2C_Moldova.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/Condri%C8%9Ba_monastery%2C_Moldova.jpg/330px-Condri%C8%9Ba_monastery%2C_Moldova.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/Condri%C8%9Ba_monastery%2C_Moldova.jpg/440px-Condri%C8%9Ba_monastery%2C_Moldova.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3082" data-file-height="2058" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Condri%C8%9Ba_monastery,_Moldova.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Moldavian <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodoxy_in_Moldova" title="Eastern Orthodoxy in Moldova">Orthodox</a> church in <a href="/wiki/Condri%C8%9Ba" title="Condrița">Condrița</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Metropolis_of_Chi%C8%99in%C4%83u_and_All_Moldova" title="Metropolis of Chișinău and All Moldova">Metropolis of Chișinău and All Moldova</a> (the Moldovan Orthodox Church), autonomous and subordinated to the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Orthodox_Church" title="Russian Orthodox Church">Russian Orthodox Church</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Metropolis_of_Bessarabia" title="Metropolis of Bessarabia">Metropolis of Bessarabia</a> (the Bessarabian Orthodox Church), autonomous and subordinated to the <a href="/wiki/Romanian_Orthodox_Church" title="Romanian Orthodox Church">Romanian Orthodox Church</a>, both claim to be the national church of the country.<sup id="cite_ref-205" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-205">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup> For the 2004 census, <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Orthodox Christians</a>, who make up 93.3% of Moldova's population, were not required to declare the particular of the two main churches they belong to. As of 2020, the U.S. <a href="/wiki/Department_of_State" class="mw-redirect" title="Department of State">Department of State</a> estimated that 90% of the Orthodox adherents belong to the Moldovan Orthodox Church.<sup id="cite_ref-206" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-206">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup> More than 2.0% of the population is Protestant including a growing number of Jehovah's Witnesses, 0.9% belongs to other religions, 1.0% is non-religious, 0.4% is atheist, and 2.2% did not answer the religion question at the census.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Education_in_Moldova" title="Education in Moldova">Education in Moldova</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Biblioteca_Nationala_Vasile_Alecsandri,_Chisinau,_Republica_Moldova_Vasile_Alecsandri_National_Library,_Chisinau,_Republic_of_Moldova_(51171201087).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/19/Biblioteca_Nationala_Vasile_Alecsandri%2C_Chisinau%2C_Republica_Moldova_Vasile_Alecsandri_National_Library%2C_Chisinau%2C_Republic_of_Moldova_%2851171201087%29.jpg/220px-Biblioteca_Nationala_Vasile_Alecsandri%2C_Chisinau%2C_Republica_Moldova_Vasile_Alecsandri_National_Library%2C_Chisinau%2C_Republic_of_Moldova_%2851171201087%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="141" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/19/Biblioteca_Nationala_Vasile_Alecsandri%2C_Chisinau%2C_Republica_Moldova_Vasile_Alecsandri_National_Library%2C_Chisinau%2C_Republic_of_Moldova_%2851171201087%29.jpg/330px-Biblioteca_Nationala_Vasile_Alecsandri%2C_Chisinau%2C_Republica_Moldova_Vasile_Alecsandri_National_Library%2C_Chisinau%2C_Republic_of_Moldova_%2851171201087%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/19/Biblioteca_Nationala_Vasile_Alecsandri%2C_Chisinau%2C_Republica_Moldova_Vasile_Alecsandri_National_Library%2C_Chisinau%2C_Republic_of_Moldova_%2851171201087%29.jpg/440px-Biblioteca_Nationala_Vasile_Alecsandri%2C_Chisinau%2C_Republica_Moldova_Vasile_Alecsandri_National_Library%2C_Chisinau%2C_Republic_of_Moldova_%2851171201087%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5689" data-file-height="3656" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Biblioteca_Nationala_Vasile_Alecsandri,_Chisinau,_Republica_Moldova_Vasile_Alecsandri_National_Library,_Chisinau,_Republic_of_Moldova_(51171201087).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/National_Library_of_Moldova" title="National Library of Moldova">National Library of Moldova</a></div></div></div>
<p>There are 16 state and 15<sup id="cite_ref-207" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-207">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup> private institutions of <a href="/wiki/Higher_education" title="Higher education">higher education</a> in Moldova, with a total of 126,100 students, including 104,300 in the state institutions and 21,700 in the private ones. The number of students per 10,000 inhabitants in Moldova has been constantly growing since the collapse of the Soviet Union, reaching 217 in 2000–2001, and 351 in 2005–2006.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/National_Library_of_Moldova" title="National Library of Moldova">National Library of Moldova</a> was founded in 1832. The <a href="/wiki/Moldova_State_University" title="Moldova State University">Moldova State University</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Academy_of_Sciences_of_Moldova" title="Academy of Sciences of Moldova">Academy of Sciences of Moldova</a>, the main scientific organizations of Moldova, were established in 1946. The Republic of Moldova was ranked 64th in the <a href="/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index" title="Global Innovation Index">Global Innovation Index</a> in 2021, down from 58th in 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-208" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-208">&#91;203&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-209" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-209">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-210" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-210">&#91;205&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-211" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-211">&#91;206&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As of 2015<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Moldova&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, Romania allocates 5,000 scholarships in high schools and universities for Moldovan students.<sup id="cite_ref-212" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-212">&#91;207&#93;</a></sup> Likewise, more than half of preschool children in Moldova benefit from Romania funded program to renovate and equip <a href="/wiki/Kindergarten" title="Kindergarten">kindergartens</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-213" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-213">&#91;208&#93;</a></sup> Almost all the population is literate: the <a href="/wiki/Literacy_rate" class="mw-redirect" title="Literacy rate">literacy rate</a> of the population aged 15 and over is estimated at 99.4% (as of 2015<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Moldova&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>).<sup id="cite_ref-214" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-214">&#91;209&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Crime">Crime</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Crime_in_Moldova" title="Crime in Moldova">Crime in Moldova</a> and <a href="/wiki/2014_Moldovan_bank_fraud_scandal" title="2014 Moldovan bank fraud scandal">2014 Moldovan bank fraud scandal</a></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/CIA_World_Factbook" class="mw-redirect" title="CIA World Factbook">CIA World Factbook</a> lists widespread <a href="/wiki/Crime" title="Crime">crime</a> and underground economic activity among major issues in Moldova.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook_Moldova_21-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook_Moldova-21">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Human_trafficking" title="Human trafficking">Human trafficking</a> of Moldovan women and children to other parts of Europe is a serious problem.<sup id="cite_ref-215" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-215">&#91;210&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-216" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-216">&#91;211&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2014, US$1 billion disappeared from three of Moldova's leading banks.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc18615_217-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc18615-217">&#91;212&#93;</a></sup> In two days, loans worth US$1 billion were transferred in to United Kingdom and <a href="/wiki/Hong_Kong" title="Hong Kong">Hong Kong</a>-registered companies whose ultimate owners are unknown.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc18615_217-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc18615-217">&#91;212&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-218" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-218">&#91;213&#93;</a></sup> Banks are administered by the <a href="/wiki/National_Bank_of_Moldova" title="National Bank of Moldova">National Bank of Moldova</a>, so this loss was covered from state reserves.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc18615_217-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc18615-217">&#91;212&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Health_and_fertility">Health and fertility</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Health_in_Moldova" class="mw-redirect" title="Health in Moldova">Health in Moldova</a></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Total_fertility_rate" title="Total fertility rate">total fertility rate</a> (TFR) in Moldova was estimated in 2015 at 1.56 children/woman,<sup id="cite_ref-219" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-219">&#91;214&#93;</a></sup> which is below the replacement rate of 2.1. In 2012, the average age of women at first birth was 23.9 years, with 75.2% of births being to women under 30, and 22.4% of births being to unmarried women.<sup id="cite_ref-220" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-220">&#91;215&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Maternal_mortality_rate" class="mw-redirect" title="Maternal mortality rate">maternal mortality rate</a> was 41 deaths/100,000 live births (in 2010)<sup id="cite_ref-221" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-221">&#91;216&#93;</a></sup> and the <a href="/wiki/Infant_mortality_rate" class="mw-redirect" title="Infant mortality rate">infant mortality rate</a> was 12.59 deaths/1,000 live births (in 2015).<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated4_222-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated4-222">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Life_expectancy" title="Life expectancy">life expectancy</a> in 2015 was estimated at 70.42 years (66.55 years male, 74.54 years female).<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated4_222-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated4-222">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Public expenditure on health was 4.2% of the GDP and private expenditure on health 3.2%.<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated1_223-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated1-223">&#91;218&#93;</a></sup> There are about 264 physicians per 100,000 people.<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated1_223-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated1-223">&#91;218&#93;</a></sup> Health expenditure was US$138 (PPP) per capita in 2004.<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated1_223-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated1-223">&#91;218&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Since the breakup of the Soviet Union, the country has seen a decrease in spending on health care and, as a result, the <a href="/wiki/Tuberculosis" title="Tuberculosis">tuberculosis</a> incidence rate in the country has grown.<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated2_224-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated2-224">&#91;219&#93;</a></sup> According to a 2009 study, Moldova was struggling with one of the highest incidence rates of multidrug-resistant tuberculosis in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated3_225-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated3-225">&#91;220&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The percentage of adults (aged 15–49) living with <a href="/wiki/HIV/AIDS" title="HIV/AIDS">HIV/AIDS</a> was estimated in 2009 at 0.40%.<sup id="cite_ref-226" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-226">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Emigration">Emigration</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Emigration_from_Moldova" title="Emigration from Moldova">Emigration from Moldova</a></div>
<p>Emigration is a mass phenomenon in Moldova and has a major impact on the country's <a href="/wiki/Demographics" class="mw-redirect" title="Demographics">demographics</a> and economy. The <a href="/wiki/Moldovan_Intelligence_and_Security_Service" class="mw-redirect" title="Moldovan Intelligence and Security Service">Moldovan Intelligence and Security Service</a> has estimated that 600,000 to one million Moldovan citizens (almost 25% of the population) are working abroad.<sup id="cite_ref-227" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-227">&#91;222&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Moldova" title="Culture of Moldova">Culture of Moldova</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"/><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:292px;max-width:292px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:145px;max-width:145px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:201px;overflow:hidden"><a href="/wiki/File:Eminescu.jpg" class="image"><img alt="Eminescu" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/Eminescu.jpg/143px-Eminescu.jpg" decoding="async" width="143" height="201" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/Eminescu.jpg/215px-Eminescu.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/Eminescu.jpg/286px-Eminescu.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="1126" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption"><a href="/wiki/Mihai_Eminescu" title="Mihai Eminescu">Mihai Eminescu</a>, the national poet of Moldova and Romania</div></div><div class="tsingle" style="width:143px;max-width:143px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:201px;overflow:hidden"><a href="/wiki/File:Dimitrie_Cantemir_-_portrait_from_Descriptio_Moldaviae,_1716.jpg" class="image"><img alt="Cantemir" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/Dimitrie_Cantemir_-_portrait_from_Descriptio_Moldaviae%2C_1716.jpg/141px-Dimitrie_Cantemir_-_portrait_from_Descriptio_Moldaviae%2C_1716.jpg" decoding="async" width="141" height="202" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/Dimitrie_Cantemir_-_portrait_from_Descriptio_Moldaviae%2C_1716.jpg/212px-Dimitrie_Cantemir_-_portrait_from_Descriptio_Moldaviae%2C_1716.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/Dimitrie_Cantemir_-_portrait_from_Descriptio_Moldaviae%2C_1716.jpg/282px-Dimitrie_Cantemir_-_portrait_from_Descriptio_Moldaviae%2C_1716.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3099" data-file-height="4448" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption"><a href="/wiki/Dimitrie_Cantemir" title="Dimitrie Cantemir">Dimitrie Cantemir</a>, Moldavian scholar of the early Enlightenment</div></div></div></div></div>
<p>Moldova's cultural tradition has been influenced primarily by the <a href="/wiki/Origin_of_the_Romanians" title="Origin of the Romanians">Romanian origins</a> of its majority population, the roots of which go back to the second century AD, the period of <a href="/wiki/Ancient_Rome" title="Ancient Rome">Roman</a> colonization in <a href="/wiki/Dacia" title="Dacia">Dacia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-culture_228-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-culture-228">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup> Located geographically at the crossroads of <a href="/wiki/Italic_peoples" title="Italic peoples">Latin</a>, <a href="/wiki/Slavic_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Slavic people">Slavic</a> and other cultures, Moldova has enriched its own culture adopting and maintaining traditions of neighbouring regions and of other influential sources.<sup id="cite_ref-229" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-229">&#91;224&#93;</a></sup> The largest ethnic group, which had come to identify itself widely as "Moldovan" by the 14th century, played a significant role in the shaping of <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Romania#Classical_age" title="Culture of Romania">classical Romanian culture</a>. The culture has been also influenced by the <a href="/wiki/Byzantine" class="mw-redirect" title="Byzantine">Byzantine</a> culture, the neighbouring Magyar and Slavic populations, and later by the Ottoman Turks. A strong Western European influence in Moldovan literature and arts was prevalent in the 19th century. During the periods 1812-1917 and 1944–89, Moldovans were influenced by Russian and Soviet administrative control as well and by ethnic Russian immigration.<sup id="cite_ref-culture_228-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-culture-228">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:International_Children%27s_Day_Celebrations_in_Chisinau,_Moldova_(7688594362_cropped).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/55/International_Children%27s_Day_Celebrations_in_Chisinau%2C_Moldova_%287688594362_cropped%29.jpg/220px-International_Children%27s_Day_Celebrations_in_Chisinau%2C_Moldova_%287688594362_cropped%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/55/International_Children%27s_Day_Celebrations_in_Chisinau%2C_Moldova_%287688594362_cropped%29.jpg/330px-International_Children%27s_Day_Celebrations_in_Chisinau%2C_Moldova_%287688594362_cropped%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/55/International_Children%27s_Day_Celebrations_in_Chisinau%2C_Moldova_%287688594362_cropped%29.jpg/440px-International_Children%27s_Day_Celebrations_in_Chisinau%2C_Moldova_%287688594362_cropped%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1313" data-file-height="877" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:International_Children%27s_Day_Celebrations_in_Chisinau,_Moldova_(7688594362_cropped).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Moldovans wearing <a href="/wiki/Romanian_dress" title="Romanian dress">national costumes</a> in <a href="/wiki/Chi%C8%99in%C4%83u" title="Chișinău">Chișinău</a></div></div></div>
<p>The country's cultural heritage was marked by numerous churches and monasteries built by the Moldavian ruler <a href="/wiki/Stephen_the_Great" title="Stephen the Great">Stephen the Great</a> in the 15th century, by the works of the later renaissance Metropolitans <a href="/wiki/Varlaam,_Metropolitan_of_Moscow" title="Varlaam, Metropolitan of Moscow">Varlaam</a> and <a href="/wiki/Dosoftei" title="Dosoftei">Dosoftei</a>, and those of scholars such as <a href="/wiki/Grigore_Ureche" title="Grigore Ureche">Grigore Ureche</a>, <a href="/wiki/Miron_Costin" title="Miron Costin">Miron Costin</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nicolae_Milescu" class="mw-redirect" title="Nicolae Milescu">Nicolae Milescu</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dimitrie_Cantemir" title="Dimitrie Cantemir">Dimitrie Cantemir</a><sup id="cite_ref-fn6_230-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-fn6-230">&#91;f&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Ion_Neculce" title="Ion Neculce">Ion Neculce</a>. In the 19th century, Moldavians from the territories of the medieval Principality of <a href="/wiki/Moldavia" title="Moldavia">Moldavia</a>, divided into <a href="/wiki/Bessarabia" title="Bessarabia">Bessarabia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bukovina" title="Bukovina">Bukovina</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Western_Moldavia" title="Western Moldavia">Western Moldavia</a> (after 1859, Romania), made a significant contribution to the formation of the modern Romanian culture. Among these were many Bessarabians, such as <a href="/wiki/Alecu_Donici" title="Alecu Donici">Alexandru Donici</a>, <a href="/wiki/Alexandru_H%C3%A2jdeu" title="Alexandru Hâjdeu">Alexandru Hâjdeu</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bogdan_Petriceicu_Hasdeu" title="Bogdan Petriceicu Hasdeu">Bogdan Petriceicu Hasdeu</a>, <a href="/wiki/Constantin_Stamati" title="Constantin Stamati">Constantin Stamati</a>, <a href="/wiki/Constantin_Stamati-Ciurea" title="Constantin Stamati-Ciurea">Constantin Stamati-Ciurea</a>, <a href="/wiki/Costache_Negruzzi" class="mw-redirect" title="Costache Negruzzi">Costache Negruzzi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Alecu_Russo" title="Alecu Russo">Alecu Russo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Constantin_Stere" title="Constantin Stere">Constantin Stere</a>.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Mihai_Eminescu" title="Mihai Eminescu">Mihai Eminescu</a>, a late <a href="/wiki/Romanticism" title="Romanticism">Romantic</a> poet, and <a href="/wiki/Ion_Creang%C4%83" title="Ion Creangă">Ion Creangă</a>, a writer, are the most influential <a href="/wiki/Romanian_language" title="Romanian language">Romanian language</a> artists, considered national writers both in Romania and Moldova.<sup id="cite_ref-231" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-231">&#91;225&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cuisine">Cuisine</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Moldovan_cuisine" title="Moldovan cuisine">Moldovan cuisine</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:M%C4%83m%C4%83lig%C4%832020-02-12.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/M%C4%83m%C4%83lig%C4%832020-02-12.jpg/220px-M%C4%83m%C4%83lig%C4%832020-02-12.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="143" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/M%C4%83m%C4%83lig%C4%832020-02-12.jpg/330px-M%C4%83m%C4%83lig%C4%832020-02-12.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/M%C4%83m%C4%83lig%C4%832020-02-12.jpg/440px-M%C4%83m%C4%83lig%C4%832020-02-12.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1079" data-file-height="701" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:M%C4%83m%C4%83lig%C4%832020-02-12.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Popular Moldovan dishes accompanied by <a href="/wiki/Sauerkraut" title="Sauerkraut">sauerkraut</a> and <a href="/wiki/M%C4%83m%C4%83lig%C4%83" title="Mămăligă">mămăligă</a> porridge.</div></div></div>
<p>Moldovan cuisine is similar to neighbouring <a href="/wiki/Romanian_cuisine" title="Romanian cuisine">Romania</a>, and has been influenced by elements of <a href="/wiki/Russian_cuisine" title="Russian cuisine">Russian</a>, <a href="/wiki/Turkish_cuisine" title="Turkish cuisine">Turkish</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Ukrainian_cuisine" title="Ukrainian cuisine">Ukrainian cuisine</a>. Main dishes include <a href="/wiki/Beef" title="Beef">beef</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pork" title="Pork">pork</a>, <a href="/wiki/Potatoes" class="mw-redirect" title="Potatoes">potatoes</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cabbage" title="Cabbage">cabbage</a>, and a variety of <a href="/wiki/Cereals" class="mw-redirect" title="Cereals">cereals</a>. Popular alcoholic beverages are <i><a href="/wiki/Moldovan_wine#Divin" title="Moldovan wine">divin</a></i> (Moldovan <a href="/wiki/Brandy" title="Brandy">brandy</a>), <a href="/wiki/Beer" title="Beer">beer</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Moldovan_wine" title="Moldovan wine">wine</a>—of which the country is known for making high-quality offerings.<sup id="cite_ref-232" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-232">&#91;226&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-233" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-233">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Traditional Moldovan dishes include <a href="/wiki/Pl%C4%83cint%C4%83" title="Plăcintă">plăcinte</a> (sweet and savoury pastries with fillings such as local cheese, cabbage, potatoes, apples, sour cherries and others), <a href="/wiki/M%C4%83m%C4%83lig%C4%83" title="Mămăligă">mămăligă</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sarmale" class="mw-redirect" title="Sarmale">sarmale</a>, and a <a href="/wiki/Chicken_soup" title="Chicken soup">chicken soup</a> called zeamă.
</p><p>Total recorded adult alcohol consumption is approximately evenly split between spirits, beer and wine. Notably, Moldova is the country with the highest alcohol consumption per capita in world, at 15.2 litres (4.0&#160;US&#160;gal) of pure alcohol imbibed in 2016.<sup id="cite_ref-234" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-234">&#91;228&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Holidays">Holidays</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Public_holidays_in_Moldova" title="Public holidays in Moldova">Public holidays in Moldova</a></div>
<p>Most retail businesses close on <a href="/wiki/New_Year%27s_Day" title="New Year&#39;s Day">New Year's Day</a> and <a href="/wiki/Independence_Day_of_the_Republic_of_Moldova" title="Independence Day of the Republic of Moldova">Independence Day</a>, but remain open on all other holidays. <a href="/wiki/Christmas" title="Christmas">Christmas</a> is celebrated either on 7 January, the traditional date in <a href="/wiki/Old_Calendarists" title="Old Calendarists">Old Calendarists</a> <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Eastern Orthodox Churches</a>, or on 25 December, with both dates being recognized as public holidays.<sup id="cite_ref-235" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-235">&#91;229&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 1 March features <i><a href="/wiki/M%C4%83r%C8%9Bi%C8%99or" title="Mărțișor">mărțișor</a></i> gifting, which is a tradition that females are gifted with a type of talisman that is given for good luck.<sup id="cite_ref-236" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-236">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Music">Music</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Music_of_Moldova" title="Music of Moldova">Music of Moldova</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Moldova_at_ESC_2011.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/25/Moldova_at_ESC_2011.jpg/220px-Moldova_at_ESC_2011.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/25/Moldova_at_ESC_2011.jpg/330px-Moldova_at_ESC_2011.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/25/Moldova_at_ESC_2011.jpg/440px-Moldova_at_ESC_2011.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1600" data-file-height="1065" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Moldova_at_ESC_2011.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Zdob_%C8%99i_Zdub" title="Zdob și Zdub">Zdob și Zdub</a> performing at the <a href="/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2011" title="Eurovision Song Contest 2011">2011 Eurovision Song Contest</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>Among Moldova's most prominent composers are <a href="/wiki/Gavriil_Musicescu" title="Gavriil Musicescu">Gavriil Musicescu</a>, <a href="/wiki/%C8%98tefan_Neaga" title="Ștefan Neaga">Ștefan Neaga</a> and <a href="/wiki/Eugen_Doga" title="Eugen Doga">Eugen Doga</a>.
</p><p>In the field of pop music, Moldova has produced the band <a href="/wiki/O-Zone" title="O-Zone">O-Zone</a>, who came to prominence in 2003, with their hit song "<a href="/wiki/Dragostea_Din_Tei" title="Dragostea Din Tei">Dragostea Din Tei</a>", which topped multiple notable single charts. Moldova has been participating in the <a href="/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest" title="Eurovision Song Contest">Eurovision Song Contest</a> since 2005. Another popular band from Moldova is <a href="/wiki/Zdob_%C8%99i_Zdub" title="Zdob și Zdub">Zdob și Zdub</a> that represented the country in the <a href="/wiki/2005_Eurovision_Song_Contest" class="mw-redirect" title="2005 Eurovision Song Contest">2005 Eurovision Song Contest</a>, finishing sixth, also in 2021, with a similar result.
</p><p>In May 2007, <a href="/wiki/Natalia_Barbu" title="Natalia Barbu">Natalia Barbu</a> represented Moldova in Helsinki at the <a href="/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2007" title="Eurovision Song Contest 2007">Eurovision Song Contest 2007</a> with her entry "<a href="/wiki/Fight_(song)" class="mw-redirect" title="Fight (song)">Fight</a>". Natalia squeezed into the final by a very small margin. She took tenth place with 109 points.
Then <a href="/wiki/Zdob_%C8%99i_Zdub" title="Zdob și Zdub">Zdob și Zdub</a> again represented Moldova in the <a href="/wiki/2011_Eurovision_Song_Contest" class="mw-redirect" title="2011 Eurovision Song Contest">2011 Eurovision Song Contest</a> finishing 12th.
</p><p>The band <a href="/wiki/SunStroke_Project" title="SunStroke Project">SunStroke Project</a> with <a href="/wiki/Olia_Tira" title="Olia Tira">Olia Tira</a> represented the country in the <a href="/wiki/2010_Eurovision_Song_Contest" class="mw-redirect" title="2010 Eurovision Song Contest">2010 Eurovision Song Contest</a> with their hit song "<a href="/wiki/Run_Away_(SunStroke_Project_and_Olia_Tira_song)" title="Run Away (SunStroke Project and Olia Tira song)">Run Away</a>". Their performance gained international notoriety as an <a href="/wiki/Internet_meme" title="Internet meme">internet meme</a> due to the pelvic thrusting and dancing of Sergey Stepanov, the band saxophonist. He has been dubbed "<a href="/wiki/Epic_Sax_Guy" class="mw-redirect" title="Epic Sax Guy">Epic Sax Guy</a>". SunStroke Project featured again in the 2017 Eurovision entry "Hey Mama" which got third place.<sup id="cite_ref-237" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-237">&#91;231&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2015 a new musical project by the name of <a href="/wiki/Carla%27s_Dreams" title="Carla&#39;s Dreams">Carla's Dreams</a> has risen in popularity around Moldova. <a href="/wiki/Carla%27s_Dreams" title="Carla&#39;s Dreams">Carla's Dreams</a> reached the top charts in multiple countries in Europe with the release of their song "Sub Pielea Mea" in 2016. The song received a lot of airplay and reached number one place on the charts in Moldova as well as Russia. The group is still active and released their latest album in 2017. The theme of the musical group is "Anonymous" as they perform with painted faces, hoodies and sunglasses. The identity of the group members is still unknown.
</p><p>Among most prominent classical musicians in Moldova are <a href="/wiki/Maria_Bie%C8%99u" title="Maria Bieșu">Maria Bieșu</a>, one of the leading world's sopranos and the winner of the Japan International Competition; pianist <a href="/wiki/Mark_Zeltser" title="Mark Zeltser">Mark Zeltser</a>, winner of the USSR National Competition, <a href="/wiki/Long-Thibaud-Crespin_Competition" title="Long-Thibaud-Crespin Competition">Long-Thibaud-Crespin Competition</a> in Paris and Busoni Competition in Bolzano, Italy.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Media">Media</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Media_of_Moldova" class="mw-redirect" title="Media of Moldova">Media of Moldova</a>, <a href="/wiki/Television_in_Moldova" title="Television in Moldova">Television in Moldova</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cinema_of_Moldova" title="Cinema of Moldova">Cinema of Moldova</a>, and <a href="/wiki/List_of_newspapers_in_Moldova" title="List of newspapers in Moldova">List of newspapers in Moldova</a></div>
<p>In October 1939, <i><a href="/wiki/Radio_Basarabia" class="mw-redirect" title="Radio Basarabia">Radio Basarabia</a></i>, a local station of the <a href="/wiki/Romanian_Radio_Broadcasting_Company" title="Romanian Radio Broadcasting Company">Romanian Radio Broadcasting Company</a>, was the first radio station opened in Chișinău. Television in Moldova was <a href="/wiki/Timeline_of_the_introduction_of_television_in_countries" title="Timeline of the introduction of television in countries">introduced in April 1958</a>, within the framework of <a href="/wiki/Soviet_television" class="mw-redirect" title="Soviet television">Soviet television</a>. Through cable, Moldovan viewers can receive a large number of <a href="/wiki/Television_in_Russia" title="Television in Russia">Russian channels</a>, a few <a href="/wiki/Television_in_Romania" title="Television in Romania">Romanian channels</a>, and several <a href="/wiki/Russian_language" title="Russian language">Russian language</a> versions of international channels in addition to several local channels.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (June 2018)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> One Russian and two local channels are aired.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (June 2018)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Infotag is the state news agency.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sports">Sports</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Sport_in_Moldova" title="Sport in Moldova">Sport in Moldova</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:AUT_vs._MDA_2015-09-05_(006).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d6/AUT_vs._MDA_2015-09-05_%28006%29.jpg/220px-AUT_vs._MDA_2015-09-05_%28006%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d6/AUT_vs._MDA_2015-09-05_%28006%29.jpg/330px-AUT_vs._MDA_2015-09-05_%28006%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d6/AUT_vs._MDA_2015-09-05_%28006%29.jpg/440px-AUT_vs._MDA_2015-09-05_%28006%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3000" data-file-height="2000" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:AUT_vs._MDA_2015-09-05_(006).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Moldova_national_football_team" title="Moldova national football team">Moldova national football team</a> in 2015</div></div></div><p><a href="/wiki/Association_football" title="Association football">Association football</a> is the most popular team sport in Moldova. The governing body is the <a href="/wiki/Moldovan_Football_Federation" title="Moldovan Football Federation">Moldovan Football Federation</a>, which belongs to <a href="/wiki/UEFA" title="UEFA">UEFA</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Moldova_national_football_team" title="Moldova national football team">Moldova national football team</a> played its first match in 1994, but never qualified to the <a href="/wiki/UEFA_European_Championship" title="UEFA European Championship">UEFA European Championship</a>. The most successful football club is <a href="/wiki/FC_Sheriff_Tiraspol" title="FC Sheriff Tiraspol">Sheriff Tiraspol</a>, the first and only Moldovan club to qualify for the group stage of the <a href="/wiki/UEFA_Champions_League" title="UEFA Champions League">Champions League</a> and the <a href="/wiki/UEFA_Europa_League" title="UEFA Europa League">Europa League</a>. Other winners of the <a href="/wiki/Moldovan_National_Division" class="mw-redirect" title="Moldovan National Division">Moldovan National Division</a> include <a href="/wiki/FC_Zimbru_Chi%C8%99in%C4%83u" title="FC Zimbru Chișinău">Zimbru Chișinău</a>, <a href="/wiki/FC_Dacia_Chi%C8%99in%C4%83u" title="FC Dacia Chișinău">Dacia Chișinău</a>, <a href="/wiki/FC_Tiraspol" title="FC Tiraspol">FC Tiraspol</a> and <a href="/wiki/FC_Milsami_Orhei" title="FC Milsami Orhei">Milsami Orhei</a>.
</p><p><i><a href="/wiki/Tr%C3%AEnt%C4%83" class="mw-redirect" title="Trîntă">Trîntă</a></i> (a form of <a href="/wiki/Wrestling" title="Wrestling">wrestling</a>) is the national sport in Moldova. <a href="/wiki/Rugby_union" title="Rugby union">Rugby union</a> is popular as well. More than 10,000 supporters turn out for home internationals. Since 2004, playing numbers at all levels have more than doubled to 3,200. Despite the hardships and deprivations the national team are ranked 34th in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-238" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-238">&#91;232&#93;</a></sup> The most prestigious <a href="/wiki/Cycling_race" class="mw-redirect" title="Cycling race">cycling race</a> is the <a href="/wiki/Moldova_President%27s_Cup" title="Moldova President&#39;s Cup">Moldova President's Cup</a>, which was first run in 2004. In chess, the Republic of Moldova has several international masters, among which can be mentioned <a href="/wiki/Viorel_Iord%C4%83chescu" title="Viorel Iordăchescu">Viorel Iordăchescu</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dmitry_Svetushkin" title="Dmitry Svetushkin">Dmitry Svetushkin</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Victor_Bologan" title="Victor Bologan">Viorel Bologan</a>.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Radu_Albot" title="Radu Albot">Radu Albot</a> is one of the most successful Moldovan tennis players, with <a href="/wiki/Association_of_Tennis_Professionals" title="Association of Tennis Professionals">ATP</a> singles (<a href="/wiki/2019_Delray_Beach_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles" title="2019 Delray Beach Open – Singles">2019 Delray Beach Open</a>) and doubles (<a href="/wiki/2015_Istanbul_Open_%E2%80%93_Doubles" title="2015 Istanbul Open – Doubles">2015 Istanbul Open</a>) titles.
</p><p>Athletes from Moldova have won European medals in <a href="/wiki/Track_and_field" title="Track and field">athletics</a>, <a href="/wiki/Biathlon" title="Biathlon">biathlon</a>, <a href="/wiki/Association_football" title="Association football">football</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Gymnastics" title="Gymnastics">gymnastics</a>; world medals in <a href="/wiki/Archery" title="Archery">archery</a>, <a href="/wiki/Judo" title="Judo">judo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Swimming_(sport)" title="Swimming (sport)">swimming</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Taekwondo" title="Taekwondo">taekwondo</a>; as well as <a href="/wiki/Olympic_Games" title="Olympic Games">Olympic</a> medals in <a href="/wiki/Boxing" title="Boxing">boxing</a>, <a href="/wiki/Canoeing" title="Canoeing">canoeing</a>, <a href="/wiki/Shooting_sports" title="Shooting sports">shooting</a>, <a href="/wiki/Olympic_weightlifting" title="Olympic weightlifting">weightlifting</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Amateur_wrestling" title="Amateur wrestling">wrestling</a>. Moldova made its Olympic debut at the <a href="/wiki/1994_Winter_Olympics" title="1994 Winter Olympics">1994 Winter Olympics</a> in Lillehammer. Olympic medalists include <a href="/wiki/Sergei_Mureiko" title="Sergei Mureiko">Sergei Mureiko</a>, <a href="/wiki/Oleg_Moldovan" title="Oleg Moldovan">Oleg Moldovan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Vitalie_Gru%C8%99ac" title="Vitalie Grușac">Vitalie Grușac</a>, <a href="/wiki/Veaceslav_Gojan" title="Veaceslav Gojan">Veaceslav Gojan</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Serghei_Tarnovschi" title="Serghei Tarnovschi">Serghei Tarnovschi</a>. <a href="/wiki/Nicolae_Juravschi" title="Nicolae Juravschi">Nicolae Juravschi</a> represented the Soviet Union at the <a href="/wiki/1988_Summer_Olympics" title="1988 Summer Olympics">1988 Seoul Games</a>, winning two medals.
</p>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist reflist-lower-alpha">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-coeli-7"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-coeli_7-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">There is <a href="/wiki/Controversy_over_ethnic_and_linguistic_identity_in_Moldova" title="Controversy over ethnic and linguistic identity in Moldova">a controversy</a> over the self-identification of Moldovans, with some authors considering them ethnic Romanians.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-11"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-11">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The <i><a href="/wiki/De_jure" title="De jure">de jure</a></i> area, accepted by the Moldovan government and the <a href="/wiki/Tiraspol" title="Tiraspol">Tiraspol</a> authorities for <a href="/wiki/Transnistria" title="Transnistria">Transnistria</a>, is 3,509.6 square kilometers.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> The <i><a href="/wiki/De_facto" title="De facto">de facto</a></i> area administered by Transnistria is 3,653 square kilometers, while the area claimed by Transnistria is 4,163 square kilometers.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-data1-13"><span class="mw-cite-backlink">^ <a href="#cite_ref-data1_13-0"><sup><i><b>a</b></i></sup></a> <a href="#cite_ref-data1_13-1"><sup><i><b>b</b></i></sup></a> <a href="#cite_ref-data1_13-2"><sup><i><b>c</b></i></sup></a> <a href="#cite_ref-data1_13-3"><sup><i><b>d</b></i></sup></a></span> <span class="reference-text">Excludes data for <a href="/wiki/Transnistria" title="Transnistria">Transnistria</a>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-fn1-46"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-fn1_46-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The Jewish minority was more numerous in the past (228,620&#160;Jews in Bessarabia in 1897, or 11.8% of the population).<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-fn2-63"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-fn2_63-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><i>Note:</i> Further 11,844 were deported on 12–13 June 1941 from other Romanian territories occupied by the USSR a year earlier.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-fn6-230"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-fn6_230-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Prince <a href="/wiki/Dimitrie_Cantemir" title="Dimitrie Cantemir">Dimitrie Cantemir</a> was one of the most important figures of the Moldavian culture of the 18th century. He wrote the first geographical, ethnographic, and economic description of the country. <span class="languageicon">(in Latin)</span> <i><a href="https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/la:Descriptio_Moldaviae" class="extiw" title="s:la:Descriptio Moldaviae">Descriptio Moldaviae</a></i>, (Berlin, 1714), at Latin Wikisource.</span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>

<li id="cite_note-200"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-200">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite class="citation web cs1 cs1-prop-foreign-lang-source"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://constcourt.md/download.php?file=cHVibGljL2NjZG9jL2hvdGFyaXJpL3JvLWhfMzZfMjAxM19yby5wZGY%3D">"Hotărâre Nr. 36 din 05.12.2013 privind interpretarea articolului 13 alin. (1) din Constituție în corelație cu Preambulul Constituției și Declarația de Independență a Republicii Moldova (Sesizările nr. 8b/2013 și 41b/2013)"</a> &#91;Decision No. 36 of 5 December 2013 regarding the interpretation of article 13 para. (1) of the Constitution in correlation with the Preamble of the Constitution and the Declaration of Independence of the Republic of Moldova (References no. 8b / 2013 and 41b / 2013)&#93; (in Romanian). Constitutional Court of the Republic of Moldova. 5 December 2013. <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://web.archive.org/web/20160305051137/http://constcourt.md/download.php?file=cHVibGljL2NjZG9jL2hvdGFyaXJpL3JvLWhfMzZfMjAxM19yby5wZGY%3D">Archived</a> from the original on 5 March 2016<span class="reference-accessdate">. Retrieved <span class="nowrap">1 September</span> 2019</span>. <q>124. [...] Prin urmare, Curtea consideră că prevederea conținută în Declarația de Independență referitoare la limba română ca limbă de stat a Republicii Moldova prevalează asupra prevederii referitoare la limba moldovenească conținute în articolul 13 al Constituției [124. [...] Therefore, the Court considers that the provision contained in the Declaration of Independence regarding the Romanian language as the state language of the Republic of Moldova prevails over the provision regarding the Moldovan language contained in Article 13 of the Constitution]</q></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=unknown&amp;rft.btitle=Hot%C4%83r%C3%A2re+Nr.+36+din+05.12.2013+privind+interpretarea+articolului+13+alin.+%281%29+din+Constitu%C8%9Bie+%C3%AEn+corela%C8%9Bie+cu+Preambulul+Constitu%C8%9Biei+%C8%99i+Declara%C8%9Bia+de+Independen%C8%9B%C4%83+a+Republicii+Moldova+%28Sesiz%C4%83rile+nr.+8b%2F2013+%C8%99i+41b%2F2013%29&amp;rft.pub=Constitutional+Court+of+the+Republic+of+Moldova&amp;rft.date=2013-12-05&amp;rft_id=http%3A%2F%2Fconstcourt.md%2Fdownload.php%3Ffile%3DcHVibGljL2NjZG9jL2hvdGFyaXJpL3JvLWhfMzZfMjAxM19yby5wZGY%253D&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3AMoldova" class="Z3988"></span></span>
</li>
